## E2E Test Infrastructure Exploration Report

I've completed a thorough exploration of the E2E test infrastructure in this codebase. Here's a comprehensive overview:

---

### **1. E2E Test Files Structure** (`cmd/entire/cli/e2e_test/`)

**Core Infrastructure Files:**

1. **setup_test.go** - TestMain initialization
   - Builds the CLI binary once before tests run
   - Determines which agent to test via `E2E_AGENT` environment variable (default: "claude-code")
   - Verifies agent availability before running tests
   - Adds binary to PATH so hooks can find it

2. **testenv.go** - TestEnv struct and helper methods (1,135 lines)
   - `NewTestEnv()` - Creates isolated test environment
   - `NewFeatureBranchEnv()` - Sets up repo with `entire enable`, ready for agent testing
   - Git operations: `GitAdd()`, `GitCommit()`, `GitCheckoutNewBranch()`, `GitStash()`, etc.
   - Agent invocation: `RunAgent()`, `RunAgentWithTools()`
   - Rewind operations: `GetRewindPoints()`, `Rewind()`
   - Checkpoint inspection: `GetLatestCheckpointID()`, `ValidateCheckpoint()`
   - File operations: `ReadFile()`, `WriteFile()`, `FileExists()`
   - Git inspection: `GetHeadHash()`, `GetCommitMessage()`, `BranchExists()`
   - Special hooks execution: `GitCommitWithShadowHooks()`, `GitCommitWithTrailerRemoved()`

3. **agent_runner.go** - AgentRunner interface for E2E agent invocation
   - Defines `AgentRunner` interface with methods: `Name()`, `IsAvailable()`, `RunPrompt()`, `RunPromptWithTools()`
   - `ClaudeCodeRunner` - Invokes Claude CLI with `--model`, `-p`, `--allowedTools` arguments
   - `GeminiCLIRunner` - Invokes Gemini CLI with `-m`, `-p`, `--approval-mode auto_edit`, `--allowed-tools`
   - Configuration via env vars: `E2E_AGENT`, `E2E_CLAUDE_MODEL`, `E2E_GEMINI_MODEL`, `E2E_TIMEOUT`
   - Default models: Claude uses "haiku" (cost efficiency), Gemini uses "gemini-2.5-flash"

4. **assertions.go** - Test assertion helpers
   - `AssertFileContains()`, `AssertFileMatches()` - Content validation
   - `AssertHelloWorldProgram()` - Validates Go hello world structure
   - `AssertCalculatorFunctions()` - Checks for expected functions
   - `AssertRewindPointExists()`, `AssertRewindPointCount()` - Rewind validation
   - `AssertCheckpointExists()`, `AssertBranchExists()` - Git state validation
   - `AssertAgentSuccess()`, `AssertExpectedFilesExist()` - Agent result validation

5. **prompts.go** - Deterministic prompt templates for E2E tests
   - `PromptCreateHelloGo` - Simple Go hello world
   - `PromptModifyHelloGo` - Modify existing file
   - `PromptCreateCalculator` - Add/Subtract functions
   - `PromptCreateConfig` - JSON config file
   - `PromptAddMultiplyFunction` - Add function to existing file
   - `PromptCreateDocs` - Markdown documentation
   - `PromptCommitChanges` - Agent commits changes
   - `PromptUseTaskTool` - Triggers subagent task checkpoints

**Test Scenario Files:**

6. **scenario_basic_workflow_test.go**
   - `TestE2E_BasicWorkflow` - Agent creates file → User commits → Checkpoint created
   - `TestE2E_MultipleChanges` - Multiple agent changes before commit

7. **scenario_checkpoint_test.go**
   - `TestE2E_CheckpointMetadata` - Metadata storage validation
   - `TestE2E_CheckpointIDFormat` - 12-hex-char checkpoint ID format
   - `TestE2E_AutoCommitStrategy` - Auto-commit strategy creates clean commits

8. **scenario_rewind_test.go**
   - `TestE2E_RewindToCheckpoint` - Restore to previous checkpoint
   - `TestE2E_RewindAfterCommit` - Shadow branch deletion after commit

9. **scenario_agent_commit_test.go**
   - `TestE2E_AgentCommitsDuringTurn` - Agent commits during its turn (deferred finalization)
   - `TestE2E_MultipleAgentSessions` - Behavior across multiple sessions

10. **scenario_subagent_test.go**
    - `TestE2E_SubagentCheckpoint` - Task tool usage and subagent checkpoints
    - Only runs on Claude Code (Task tool is Claude-specific)

11. **scenario_checkpoint_workflows_test.go**
    - `TestE2E_Scenario3_MultipleGranularCommits` - Multiple commits during one turn

12. **resume_relocated_repo_test.go**
    - `TestE2E_ResumeInRelocatedRepo` - Location-independent transcript resolution
    - Validates that paths are computed dynamically from current repo location

### **2. Agent Implementations** (`cmd/entire/cli/agent/`)

**Core Agent System:**

**registry.go** - Agent registration and discovery
- Global registry with thread-safe RWMutex
- `Register(name, factory)` - Called from each agent's init()
- `Get(name)` - Retrieve agent by name
- `List()` - All registered agents in sorted order
- `Detect()` / `DetectAll()` - Auto-detect agents in repository
- `GetByAgentType()` - Look up by type identifier

**agent.go** - Agent interface definition
- **Identity methods** (5): `Name()`, `Type()`, `Description()`, `IsPreview()`, `DetectPresence()`, `ProtectedDirs()`
- **Event mapping** (2): `HookNames()`, `ParseHookEvent()`
- **Transcript storage** (3): `ReadTranscript()`, `ChunkTranscript()`, `ReassembleTranscript()`
- **Legacy methods** (8): `GetSessionID()`, `GetSessionDir()`, `ResolveSessionFile()`, `ReadSession()`, `WriteSession()`, `FormatResumeCommand()`

Optional interfaces:
- `HookSupport` - For agents with hook installation (Claude Code, OpenCode)
- `FileWatcher` - For agents without hooks (Aider)
- `TranscriptAnalyzer` - Extract prompts, files, summaries from transcripts
- `TokenCalculator` - Compute token usage from transcripts

**types.go** - Hook types and data structures
- `HookType`: "session_start", "session_end", "user_prompt_submit", "stop", "pre_tool_use", "post_tool_use"
- `HookInput` - Normalized hook data (SessionID, SessionRef, UserPrompt, ToolName, ToolUseID, etc.)
- `TokenUsage` - InputTokens, OutputTokens, CacheReadTokens, CacheCreationTokens, APICallCount

---

#### **Claude Code Agent** (`cmd/entire/cli/agent/claudecode/`)

**claude.go** - Main Claude Code agent implementation
- Transcript format: JSONL (one JSON object per line)
- Session directory: `~/.claude/projects/<sanitized-repo-path>/`
- Protected dirs: `.claude`
- Hook names: session-start, session-end, stop, user-prompt-submit, pre-task, post-task, post-todo
- Type identifier: "Claude Code"

**hooks.go** - Hook installation in `.claude/settings.json`
- Installs 4 main hooks (SessionStart, SessionEnd, Stop, UserPromptSubmit)
- Pre-task and Post-task hooks for subagent support
- Adds metadata deny rule: `Read(./.entire/metadata/**)`
- Handles force reinstall and idempotent updates

**transcript.go** - JSONL transcript parsing and analysis
- `SliceFromLine()` - Get transcript from line offset
- `GetTranscriptPosition()` - Line count
- `ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset()` - Files modified by tool calls
- `ExtractPrompts()` - User prompts from transcript
- `ExtractSummary()` - Last assistant message
- `CalculateTokenUsage()` - Token statistics

**lifecycle.go** - Hook event parsing
- Converts Claude hooks to normalized Agent Events
- Pre-prompt state capture (before UserPromptSubmit)
- Handles tool use hooks for subagent checkpoints

---

#### **Gemini CLI Agent** (`cmd/entire/cli/agent/geminicli/`)

**gemini.go** - Main Gemini CLI agent implementation
- Transcript format: JSON with messages array
- Session directory: `~/.gemini/sessions/`
- Protected dirs: `.gemini`
- Type identifier: "Gemini CLI"
- IsPreview: true

**transcript.go** - JSON transcript parsing
- Similar interface to Claude but for JSON format
- Message-based instead of line-based offsets

**lifecycle.go** - Hook event parsing for Gemini

---

#### **OpenCode Agent** (`cmd/entire/cli/agent/opencode/`)

**opencode.go** - Main OpenCode agent implementation
- Transcript format: JSON export from `opencode export` command
- Session directory: `os.TempDir()/entire-opencode/<sanitized-path>/`
- Protected dirs: `.opencode`
- Type identifier: "OpenCode"
- IsPreview: true
- Uses plugin-based hook system (TypeScript plugin in `.opencode/plugins/entire.ts`)

**hooks.go** - Plugin hook installation
- Generates `.opencode/plugins/entire.ts` plugin file
- Idempotent installation with force option
- Supported hooks: session-start, session-end, turn-start, turn-end, compaction

**lifecycle.go** - Hook event parsing
- Maps OpenCode hooks to normalized events
- `fetchAndCacheExport()` - Runs `opencode export` to get transcript
- Caches exported sessions to temp files

**transcript.go** - OpenCode export JSON parsing
- `ExportSession` - Top-level structure with Info + Messages
- `ExportMessage` - Message metadata and parts
- File modification tools: edit, write, patch
- `GetTranscriptPosition()` - Message count
- `ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset()` - Files from tool calls
- `ExtractPrompts()`, `ExtractSummary()` - Content extraction
- `CalculateTokenUsage()` - Token statistics from message Tokens field

**cli_commands.go** - OpenCode CLI wrapper
- `runOpenCodeExport()` - Get session data as JSON
- `runOpenCodeSessionDelete()` - Delete session from database
- `runOpenCodeImport()` - Restore session to database (for rewind)

**types.go** - OpenCode JSON type definitions
- `ExportSession`, `SessionInfo`, `ExportMessage`, `MessageInfo`
- `Part` - Message content (text, tool, etc.)
- `ToolState` - Tool execution details with input/output
- `Tokens` - Token usage with cache breakdown

---

### **3. Mise Configuration** (mise.toml)

**E2E Testing Tasks:**

```
[tasks."test:e2e"]
run = "go test -tags=e2e -count=1 -timeout=30m -v ./cmd/entire/cli/e2e_test/..."

[tasks."test:e2e:claude"]
run = "E2E_AGENT=claude-code go test -tags=e2e -count=1 -timeout=30m -v ./cmd/entire/cli/e2e_test/..."

[tasks."test:e2e:gemini"]
run = "E2E_AGENT=gemini go test -tags=e2e -count=1 -parallel 1 -timeout=30m -v ./cmd/entire/cli/e2e_test/..."
```

**Key Flags:**
- `-tags=e2e` - Only includes `//go:build e2e` test files
- `-count=1` - Disables test caching (E2E tests make real API calls)
- `-timeout=30m` - 30-minute timeout for all tests
- `-parallel 1` - Gemini tests run sequentially (rate limit avoidance)
- `-v` - Verbose output

**Environment Variables:**
- `E2E_AGENT` - Which agent to test (claude-code, gemini, opencode)
- `E2E_CLAUDE_MODEL` - Claude model (default: haiku)
- `E2E_GEMINI_MODEL` - Gemini model (default: gemini-2.5-flash)
- `E2E_TIMEOUT` - Timeout per prompt (default: 2m)

---

### **4. How Agents Are Selected and Configured**

**Selection Process (setup_test.go):**
1. Check `E2E_AGENT` environment variable
2. Default to "claude-code" if not set
3. Call `NewAgentRunner(agentName, config)` to create runner
4. Check `IsAvailable()` - verifies CLI is installed and working
5. Skip tests with message if agent unavailable (non-fatal)

**Configuration:**
- `AgentRunnerConfig` struct: Model, Timeout
- Environment variables override defaults
- Each agent runner reads config from env or uses hardcoded defaults

**Available Agents in TestEnv:**
```go
const AgentNameClaudeCode = "claude-code"
const AgentNameGemini = "gemini"
```

**NewAgentRunner Factory:**
```go
func NewAgentRunner(name string, config AgentRunnerConfig) AgentRunner {
    switch name {
    case AgentNameClaudeCode:
        return NewClaudeCodeRunner(config)
    case AgentNameGemini:
        return NewGeminiCLIRunner(config)
    default:
        return &unavailableRunner{name: name}  // Reports as unavailable
    }
}
```

---

### **5. Key Testing Patterns**

**Parallel Testing:**
- All test functions use `t.Parallel()` for concurrent execution
- Except Gemini tests which run with `-parallel 1` due to rate limiting
- TestMain runs once before parallel tests (agent availability check, binary build)

**Test Organization:**
- Scenario tests grouped by feature (basic workflow, checkpoints, rewind, etc.)
- Prompt templates for deterministic outputs
- Assertion helpers for common validations
- Helper methods in TestEnv for repetitive operations

**Hook Simulation:**
- `GitCommitWithShadowHooks()` - Runs prepare-commit-msg and post-commit hooks
- Simulates real workflow with hook file modification
- Special env vars for test agent project directories

**Checkpoint Validation:**
- `ValidateCheckpoint()` - Comprehensive metadata validation
- Checks sharded path format, JSONL format, hash verification
- Works with both strategies (manual-commit, auto-commit)

**Rewind Testing:**
- Get rewind points before and after operations
- Verify shadow branch deletion after commit
- Confirm file restoration from checkpoints
- Check that logs-only points replace shadow branch points

---

### **6. Agent-Specific Implementation Details**

**Claude Code:**
- OAuth authentication via `claude login`
- Stores projects in `~/.claude/projects/`
- JSONL transcript format (one JSON per line)
- Supports both hooks and deprecated settings approach
- Pre-task/Post-task hooks for subagent support

**Gemini CLI:**
- Google API authentication
- Stores sessions in `~/.gemini/sessions/`
- JSON messages array format
- Approval mode: auto_edit for write operations
- Pre-view (experimental integration)

**OpenCode:**
- TypeScript plugin hook system
- Stores sessions in temp directory (ephemeral)
- JSON export format with sophisticated message structure
- Database-backed: `opencode export`, `opencode import`, `opencode session delete`
- Token usage breakdown (input, output, cache read, cache write)
- First-class support for file tool details (edit, write, patch)
- Rewind support via database deletion + reimport

---

### **7. Critical Implementation Patterns**

**Transcript Chunking:**
- Agents handle large transcripts via chunking for storage
- Format-aware: JSONL splits at line boundaries, JSON splits message arrays
- Reassembly is format-specific

**Location-Independent Transcript Resolution:**
- Transcript paths computed dynamically from current repo location
- Never stored in checkpoint metadata
- Enables rewind/resume after repo relocation

**Token Usage Calculation:**
- Per-agent implementation in TranscriptAnalyzer interface
- OpenCode extracts from message metadata
- Claude/Gemini aggregate from tool invocations

**Multi-Session Behavior:**
- Each session gets unique ID
- Concurrent sessions in same directory create separate checkpoints
- Subagent task checkpoints marked with `is_task_checkpoint=true`

---

This infrastructure provides a complete framework for testing Entire CLI against real coding agents with comprehensive checkpoint and rewind verification.

    22→// AgentRunner abstracts invoking a coding agent for e2e tests.
    23→// This follows the multi-agent pattern from cmd/entire/cli/agent/agent.go.
    24→type AgentRunner interface {
    25→	// Name returns the agent name (e.g., "claude-code", "gemini-cli")
    26→	Name() string
    27→
    28→	// IsAvailable checks if the agent CLI is installed and authenticated
    29→	IsAvailable() (bool, error)

    31→	// RunPrompt executes a prompt and returns the result
    32→	RunPrompt(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string) (*AgentResult, error)

    34→	// RunPromptWithTools executes with specific allowed tools
    35→	RunPromptWithTools(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string, tools []string) (*AgentResult, error)
    36→}
    37→
    38→// AgentResult holds the result of an agent invocation.
    39→type AgentResult struct {
    40→	Stdout   string
    41→	Stderr   string
    42→	ExitCode int
    43→	Duration time.Duration
    44→}
    45→
    46→// AgentRunnerConfig holds configuration for agent runners.

    49→	Timeout time.Duration // Timeout per prompt
    50→}
    51→
    52→// NewAgentRunner creates an agent runner based on the agent name.
    53→//
    54→//nolint:ireturn // factory pattern intentionally returns interface
    55→func NewAgentRunner(name string, config AgentRunnerConfig) AgentRunner {

    58→		return NewClaudeCodeRunner(config)
    59→	case AgentNameGemini:
    60→		return NewGeminiCLIRunner(config)
    61→	default:
    62→		// Return a runner that reports as unavailable
    63→		return &unavailableRunner{name: name}
    64→	}
    65→}

    70→}

    85→
    86→// ClaudeCodeRunner implements AgentRunner for Claude Code CLI.
    87→type ClaudeCodeRunner struct {
    88→	Model        string
    89→	Timeout      time.Duration
    90→	AllowedTools []string
    91→}
    92→
    93→// NewClaudeCodeRunner creates a new Claude Code runner with the given config.
    94→func NewClaudeCodeRunner(config AgentRunnerConfig) *ClaudeCodeRunner {
    95→	model := config.Model
    96→	if model == "" {
    97→		model = os.Getenv("E2E_CLAUDE_MODEL")
    98→		if model == "" {
    99→			model = "haiku"
   100→		}
   101→	}
   102→
   103→	timeout := config.Timeout
   104→	if timeout == 0 {
   105→		if envTimeout := os.Getenv("E2E_TIMEOUT"); envTimeout != "" {
   106→			if parsed, err := time.ParseDuration(envTimeout); err == nil {
   107→				timeout = parsed
   108→			}
   109→		}
   110→		if timeout == 0 {
   111→			timeout = 2 * time.Minute
   112→		}
   113→	}
   114→
   115→	return &ClaudeCodeRunner{
   116→		Model:        model,
   117→		Timeout:      timeout,
   118→		AllowedTools: []string{"Edit", "Read", "Write", "Bash", "Glob", "Grep"},
   119→	}
   120→}
   121→
   122→func (r *ClaudeCodeRunner) Name() string {
   123→	return AgentNameClaudeCode
   124→}
   125→
   126→// IsAvailable checks if Claude CLI is installed and responds to --version.
   127→// Note: This does NOT verify authentication status. Claude Code uses OAuth
   128→// authentication (via `claude login`), not ANTHROPIC_API_KEY. If the CLI is
   129→// installed but not logged in, tests will fail at RunPrompt time.
   130→func (r *ClaudeCodeRunner) IsAvailable() (bool, error) {
   131→	// Check if claude CLI is in PATH
   132→	if _, err := exec.LookPath("claude"); err != nil {
   133→		return false, fmt.Errorf("claude CLI not found in PATH: %w", err)
   134→	}
   135→
   136→	// Check if claude is working (--version doesn't require auth)
   137→	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 10*time.Second)
   138→	defer cancel()
   139→
   140→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "claude", "--version")
   141→	if err := cmd.Run(); err != nil {
   142→		return false, fmt.Errorf("claude CLI not working: %w", err)
   143→	}
   144→
   145→	return true, nil
   146→}
   147→
   148→func (r *ClaudeCodeRunner) RunPrompt(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string) (*AgentResult, error) {
   149→	return r.RunPromptWithTools(ctx, workDir, prompt, r.AllowedTools)
   150→}
   151→
   152→func (r *ClaudeCodeRunner) RunPromptWithTools(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string, tools []string) (*AgentResult, error) {
   153→	// Build command: claude --model <model> -p "<prompt>" --allowedTools <tools>
   154→	args := []string{
   155→		"--model", r.Model,
   156→		"-p", prompt,
   157→	}
   158→
   159→	if len(tools) > 0 {
   160→		// Claude CLI expects each tool as a separate argument after --allowedTools
   161→		// e.g., --allowedTools Edit Read Bash (not --allowedTools "Edit,Read,Bash")
   162→		args = append(args, "--allowedTools")
   163→		args = append(args, tools...)
   164→	}
   165→
   166→	// Create context with timeout
   167→	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, r.Timeout)
   168→	defer cancel()
   169→
   170→	//nolint:gosec // args are constructed from trusted config, not user input
   171→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "claude", args...)
   172→	cmd.Dir = workDir
   173→
   174→	var stdout, stderr bytes.Buffer
   175→	cmd.Stdout = &stdout
   176→	cmd.Stderr = &stderr
   177→
   178→	start := time.Now()
   179→	err := cmd.Run()
   180→	duration := time.Since(start)
   181→
   182→	result := &AgentResult{
   183→		Stdout:   stdout.String(),
   184→		Stderr:   stderr.String(),
   185→		Duration: duration,
   186→	}
   187→
   188→	if err != nil {
   189→		exitErr := &exec.ExitError{}
   190→		if errors.As(err, &exitErr) {
   191→			result.ExitCode = exitErr.ExitCode()
   192→		} else {
   193→			result.ExitCode = -1
   194→		}
   195→		//nolint:wrapcheck // error is from exec.Run, caller can check ExitCode in result
   196→		return result, err
   197→	}
   198→
   199→	result.ExitCode = 0
   200→	return result, nil
   201→}
   202→
   203→// GeminiCLIRunner implements AgentRunner for Gemini CLI.
   204→// See: https://github.com/google-gemini/gemini-cli
   205→type GeminiCLIRunner struct {
   206→	Model   string
   207→	Timeout time.Duration
   208→}
   209→
   210→// NewGeminiCLIRunner creates a new Gemini CLI runner with the given config.
   211→func NewGeminiCLIRunner(config AgentRunnerConfig) *GeminiCLIRunner {
   212→	model := config.Model
   213→	if model == "" {
   214→		model = os.Getenv("E2E_GEMINI_MODEL")
   215→		// Default to gemini-2.5-flash if not specified
   216→		if model == "" {
   217→			model = "gemini-2.5-flash"
   218→		}
   219→	}
   220→
   221→	timeout := config.Timeout
   222→	if timeout == 0 {
   223→		if envTimeout := os.Getenv("E2E_TIMEOUT"); envTimeout != "" {
   224→			if parsed, err := time.ParseDuration(envTimeout); err == nil {
   225→				timeout = parsed
   226→			}
   227→		}
   228→		if timeout == 0 {
   229→			timeout = 2 * time.Minute
   230→		}
   231→	}
   232→
   233→	return &GeminiCLIRunner{
   234→		Model:   model,
   235→		Timeout: timeout,
   236→	}
   237→}
   238→
   239→func (r *GeminiCLIRunner) Name() string {
   240→	return AgentNameGemini
   241→}
   242→
   243→// IsAvailable checks if Gemini CLI is installed and authenticated.
   244→func (r *GeminiCLIRunner) IsAvailable() (bool, error) {
   245→	// Check if gemini CLI is in PATH
   246→	if _, err := exec.LookPath("gemini"); err != nil {
   247→		return false, fmt.Errorf("gemini CLI not found in PATH: %w", err)
   248→	}
   249→
   250→	// Check if gemini is working (--version doesn't require auth)
   251→	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 10*time.Second)
   252→	defer cancel()
   253→
   254→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "gemini", "--version")
   255→	if err := cmd.Run(); err != nil {
   256→		return false, fmt.Errorf("gemini CLI not working: %w", err)
   257→	}
   258→
   259→	return true, nil
   260→}
   261→
   262→func (r *GeminiCLIRunner) RunPrompt(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string) (*AgentResult, error) {
   263→	return r.RunPromptWithTools(ctx, workDir, prompt, nil)
   264→}
   265→
   266→func (r *GeminiCLIRunner) RunPromptWithTools(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string, tools []string) (*AgentResult, error) {
   267→	// Build command: gemini -m <model> -p "<prompt>" --approval-mode auto_edit --allowed-tools <tools>
   268→	// --approval-mode auto_edit: auto-approves edit tools (write_file, replace) while prompting for others
   269→	// --allowed-tools: comma-separated list of tools that bypass confirmation (e.g., git commands)
   270→	args := []string{
   271→		"-m", r.Model,
   272→		"-p", prompt,
   273→		"--approval-mode", "auto_edit",
   274→	}
   275→
   276→	// Add default git tools that should be allowed without confirmation
   277→	defaultAllowedTools := []string{
   278→		"ShellTool(git status)",
   279→		"ShellTool(git add)",
   280→		"ShellTool(git commit)",
   281→		"ShellTool(git diff)",
   282→		"ShellTool(git log)",
   283→	}
   284→
   285→	// Merge with any additional tools passed in
   286→	allTools := append(defaultAllowedTools, tools...)
   287→	if len(allTools) > 0 {
   288→		args = append(args, "--allowed-tools", strings.Join(allTools, ","))
   289→	}
   290→
   291→	// Create context with timeout
   292→	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, r.Timeout)
   293→	defer cancel()
   294→
   295→	//nolint:gosec // args are constructed from trusted config, not user input
   296→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "gemini", args...)
   297→	cmd.Dir = workDir
   298→
   299→	var stdout, stderr bytes.Buffer
   300→	cmd.Stdout = &stdout
   301→	cmd.Stderr = &stderr
   302→
   303→	start := time.Now()
   304→	err := cmd.Run()
   305→	duration := time.Since(start)
   306→
   307→	result := &AgentResult{
   308→		Stdout:   stdout.String(),
   309→		Stderr:   stderr.String(),
   310→		Duration: duration,
   311→	}
   312→
   313→	if err != nil {
   314→		exitErr := &exec.ExitError{}
   315→		if errors.As(err, &exitErr) {
   316→			result.ExitCode = exitErr.ExitCode()
   317→		} else {
   318→			result.ExitCode = -1
   319→		}
   320→		//nolint:wrapcheck // error is from exec.Run, caller can check ExitCode in result
   321→		return result, err
   322→	}
   323→
   324→	result.ExitCode = 0
   325→	return result, nil
   326→}
   327→

    14→
    15→// TestMain builds the CLI binary once and checks agent availability before running tests.
    16→func TestMain(m *testing.M) {
    17→	// Determine which agent to test with
    18→	defaultAgent = os.Getenv("E2E_AGENT")
    19→	if defaultAgent == "" {
    20→		defaultAgent = AgentNameClaudeCode
    21→	}
    22→
    23→	// Check if the agent is available
    24→	runner := NewAgentRunner(defaultAgent, AgentRunnerConfig{})
    25→	available, err := runner.IsAvailable()
    26→	if !available {
    27→		fmt.Printf("Agent %s not available (%v), skipping E2E tests\n", defaultAgent, err)
    28→		// Exit 0 to not fail CI when agent isn't configured
    29→		os.Exit(0)
    30→	}
    31→
    32→	// Build binary once to a temp directory
    33→	tmpDir, err := os.MkdirTemp("", "entire-e2e-test-*")
    34→	if err != nil {
    35→		fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "failed to create temp dir for binary: %v\n", err)
    36→		os.Exit(1)
    37→	}
    38→
    39→	testBinaryPath = filepath.Join(tmpDir, "entire")
    40→
    41→	moduleRoot := findModuleRoot()
    42→	ctx := context.Background()
    43→
    44→	buildCmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "go", "build", "-o", testBinaryPath, ".")
    45→	buildCmd.Dir = filepath.Join(moduleRoot, "cmd", "entire")
    46→
    47→	buildOutput, err := buildCmd.CombinedOutput()
    48→	if err != nil {
    49→		fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "failed to build CLI binary: %v\nOutput: %s\n", err, buildOutput)
    50→		os.RemoveAll(tmpDir)
    51→		os.Exit(1)
    52→	}
    53→
    54→	// Add binary to PATH so hooks can find it.
    55→	// This is safe because:
    56→	// 1. TestMain runs once before any tests (no parallel conflict with os.Setenv)
    57→	// 2. Each test package gets its own TestMain execution
    58→	// 3. PATH is restored in the same TestMain after m.Run() completes
    59→	// 4. The binary path is unique per test run (temp dir)
    60→	origPath := os.Getenv("PATH")
    61→	os.Setenv("PATH", tmpDir+string(os.PathListSeparator)+origPath)
    62→
    63→	// Run tests
    64→	code := m.Run()
    65→
    66→	// Cleanup
    67→	os.Setenv("PATH", origPath)
    68→	os.RemoveAll(tmpDir)
    69→	os.Exit(code)
    70→}
    71→
    72→// findModuleRoot finds the Go module root by walking up from the current file.
    73→func findModuleRoot() string {
    74→	_, thisFile, _, ok := runtime.Caller(0)
    75→	if !ok {
    76→		panic("failed to get current file path via runtime.Caller")
    77→	}
    78→	dir := filepath.Dir(thisFile)
    79→
    80→	for {
    81→		if _, err := os.Stat(filepath.Join(dir, "go.mod")); err == nil {
    82→			return dir
    83→		}
    84→		parent := filepath.Dir(dir)
    85→		if parent == dir {
    86→			panic("could not find go.mod starting from " + thisFile)
    87→		}
    88→		dir = parent
    89→	}
    90→}
    91→

     1→// Package opencode implements the Agent interface for OpenCode.
     2→package opencode
     3→
     4→import (
     5→	"context"
     6→	"encoding/json"
     7→	"errors"
     8→	"fmt"
     9→	"log/slog"
    10→	"os"
    11→	"path/filepath"
    12→	"regexp"
    13→	"strings"
    14→
    15→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent"
    16→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/logging"
    17→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/paths"
    18→)
    19→
    20→//nolint:gochecknoinits // Agent self-registration is the intended pattern
    21→func init() {
    22→	agent.Register(agent.AgentNameOpenCode, NewOpenCodeAgent)
    23→}
    24→
    25→//nolint:revive // OpenCodeAgent is clearer than Agent in this context
    26→type OpenCodeAgent struct{}
    27→
    28→// NewOpenCodeAgent creates a new OpenCode agent instance.
    29→func NewOpenCodeAgent() agent.Agent {
    30→	return &OpenCodeAgent{}
    31→}
    32→
    33→// --- Identity ---
    34→
    35→func (a *OpenCodeAgent) Name() agent.AgentName   { return agent.AgentNameOpenCode }
    36→func (a *OpenCodeAgent) Type() agent.AgentType   { return agent.AgentTypeOpenCode }
    37→func (a *OpenCodeAgent) Description() string     { return "OpenCode - AI-powered terminal coding agent" }
    38→func (a *OpenCodeAgent) IsPreview() bool         { return true }
    39→func (a *OpenCodeAgent) ProtectedDirs() []string { return []string{".opencode"} }
    40→
    41→func (a *OpenCodeAgent) DetectPresence() (bool, error) {
    42→	repoRoot, err := paths.RepoRoot()
    43→	if err != nil {
    44→		repoRoot = "."
    45→	}
    46→	// Check for .opencode directory or opencode.json config
    47→	if _, err := os.Stat(filepath.Join(repoRoot, ".opencode")); err == nil {
    48→		return true, nil
    49→	}
    50→	if _, err := os.Stat(filepath.Join(repoRoot, "opencode.json")); err == nil {
    51→		return true, nil
    52→	}
    53→	return false, nil
    54→}
    55→
    56→// --- Transcript Storage ---
    57→
    58→// ReadTranscript reads the transcript for a session.
    59→// The sessionRef is expected to be a path to the export JSON file.
    60→func (a *OpenCodeAgent) ReadTranscript(sessionRef string) ([]byte, error) {
    61→	data, err := os.ReadFile(sessionRef) //nolint:gosec // Path from agent hook
    62→	if err != nil {
    63→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to read opencode transcript: %w", err)
    64→	}
    65→	return data, nil
    66→}
    67→
    68→// ChunkTranscript splits an OpenCode export JSON transcript by distributing messages across chunks.
    69→// OpenCode uses JSON format with {"info": {...}, "messages": [...]} structure.
    70→func (a *OpenCodeAgent) ChunkTranscript(content []byte, maxSize int) ([][]byte, error) {
    71→	var session ExportSession
    72→	if err := json.Unmarshal(content, &session); err != nil {
    73→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to parse export session for chunking: %w", err)
    74→	}
    75→
    76→	if len(session.Messages) == 0 {
    77→		return [][]byte{content}, nil
    78→	}
    79→
    80→	// Marshal info to calculate accurate base size
    81→	infoBytes, err := json.Marshal(session.Info)
    82→	if err != nil {
    83→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal session info for chunking: %w", err)
    84→	}
    85→	// Base JSON structure size: {"info":<info>,"messages":[ ... ]}
    86→	baseSize := len(`{"info":`) + len(infoBytes) + len(`,"messages":[]}`)
    87→
    88→	var chunks [][]byte
    89→	var currentMessages []ExportMessage
    90→	currentSize := baseSize
    91→
    92→	for _, msg := range session.Messages {
    93→		// Marshal message to get its size
    94→		msgBytes, err := json.Marshal(msg)
    95→		if err != nil {
    96→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal message for chunking: %w", err)
    97→		}
    98→		msgSize := len(msgBytes) + 1 // +1 for comma separator
    99→
   100→		if currentSize+msgSize > maxSize && len(currentMessages) > 0 {
   101→			// Save current chunk
   102→			chunkData, err := json.Marshal(ExportSession{Info: session.Info, Messages: currentMessages})
   103→			if err != nil {
   104→				return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal chunk: %w", err)
   105→			}
   106→			chunks = append(chunks, chunkData)
   107→
   108→			// Start new chunk
   109→			currentMessages = nil
   110→			currentSize = baseSize
   111→		}
   112→
   113→		currentMessages = append(currentMessages, msg)
   114→		currentSize += msgSize
   115→	}
   116→
   117→	// Add the last chunk
   118→	if len(currentMessages) > 0 {
   119→		chunkData, err := json.Marshal(ExportSession{Info: session.Info, Messages: currentMessages})
   120→		if err != nil {
   121→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal final chunk: %w", err)
   122→		}
   123→		chunks = append(chunks, chunkData)
   124→	}
   125→
   126→	if len(chunks) == 0 {
   127→		return nil, errors.New("failed to create any chunks")
   128→	}
   129→
   130→	return chunks, nil
   131→}
   132→
   133→// ReassembleTranscript merges OpenCode export JSON chunks by combining their message arrays.
   134→func (a *OpenCodeAgent) ReassembleTranscript(chunks [][]byte) ([]byte, error) {
   135→	if len(chunks) == 0 {
   136→		return nil, errors.New("no chunks to reassemble")
   137→	}
   138→
   139→	var allMessages []ExportMessage
   140→	var sessionInfo SessionInfo
   141→
   142→	for i, chunk := range chunks {
   143→		var session ExportSession
   144→		if err := json.Unmarshal(chunk, &session); err != nil {
   145→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to unmarshal chunk %d: %w", i, err)
   146→		}
   147→		if i == 0 {
   148→			sessionInfo = session.Info // Preserve session info from first chunk
   149→		}
   150→		allMessages = append(allMessages, session.Messages...)
   151→	}
   152→
   153→	result, err := json.Marshal(ExportSession{Info: sessionInfo, Messages: allMessages})
   154→	if err != nil {
   155→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal reassembled transcript: %w", err)
   156→	}
   157→	return result, nil
   158→}
   159→
   160→// --- Legacy methods ---
   161→
   162→func (a *OpenCodeAgent) GetSessionID(input *agent.HookInput) string {
   163→	return input.SessionID
   164→}
   165→
   166→// GetSessionDir returns the directory where Entire stores OpenCode session transcripts.
   167→// Transcripts are ephemeral handoff files between the TS plugin and the Go hook handler.
   168→// Once checkpointed, the data lives on git refs and the file is disposable.
   169→// Stored in os.TempDir()/entire-opencode/<sanitized-path>/ to avoid squatting on
   170→// OpenCode's own directories (~/.opencode/ is project-level, not home-level).
   171→func (a *OpenCodeAgent) GetSessionDir(repoPath string) (string, error) {
   172→	// Check for test environment override
   173→	if override := os.Getenv("ENTIRE_TEST_OPENCODE_PROJECT_DIR"); override != "" {
   174→		return override, nil
   175→	}
   176→
   177→	projectDir := SanitizePathForOpenCode(repoPath)
   178→	return filepath.Join(os.TempDir(), "entire-opencode", projectDir), nil
   179→}
   180→
   181→func (a *OpenCodeAgent) ResolveSessionFile(sessionDir, agentSessionID string) string {
   182→	return filepath.Join(sessionDir, agentSessionID+".json")
   183→}
   184→
   185→func (a *OpenCodeAgent) ReadSession(input *agent.HookInput) (*agent.AgentSession, error) {
   186→	if input.SessionRef == "" {
   187→		return nil, errors.New("no session ref provided")
   188→	}
   189→	data, err := os.ReadFile(input.SessionRef)
   190→	if err != nil {
   191→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to read session: %w", err)
   192→	}
   193→
   194→	// Parse to extract computed fields
   195→	modifiedFiles, err := ExtractModifiedFiles(data)
   196→	if err != nil {
   197→		// Non-fatal: we can still return the session without modified files
   198→		modifiedFiles = nil
   199→	}
   200→
   201→	return &agent.AgentSession{
   202→		AgentName:     a.Name(),
   203→		SessionID:     input.SessionID,
   204→		SessionRef:    input.SessionRef,
   205→		NativeData:    data,
   206→		ModifiedFiles: modifiedFiles,
   207→	}, nil
   208→}
   209→
   210→func (a *OpenCodeAgent) WriteSession(session *agent.AgentSession) error {
   211→	if session == nil {
   212→		return errors.New("nil session")
   213→	}
   214→	if len(session.NativeData) == 0 {
   215→		return errors.New("no session data to write")
   216→	}
   217→
   218→	// Import the session into OpenCode's database.
   219→	// This enables `opencode -s <id>` for both resume and rewind.
   220→	return a.importSessionIntoOpenCode(session.SessionID, session.NativeData)
   221→}
   222→
   223→// importSessionIntoOpenCode writes the export JSON to a temp file and runs
   224→// `opencode import` to restore the session into OpenCode's database.
   225→// For rewind (session already exists), the session is deleted first so the
   226→// reimport replaces it with the checkpoint-state messages.
   227→func (a *OpenCodeAgent) importSessionIntoOpenCode(sessionID string, exportData []byte) error {
   228→	// Delete existing session first so reimport replaces it cleanly.
   229→	// opencode import uses ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING, so existing messages
   230→	// would be skipped without this step (breaking rewind).
   231→	if err := runOpenCodeSessionDelete(sessionID); err != nil {
   232→		// Non-fatal: session might not exist yet (first session).
   233→		// Import will still work for new sessions; only rewind of existing sessions
   234→		// would have stale messages.
   235→		logging.Warn(context.Background(), "could not delete existing opencode session",
   236→			slog.String("session_id", sessionID),
   237→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   238→		)
   239→	}
   240→
   241→	// Write export JSON to a temp file for opencode import
   242→	tmpFile, err := os.CreateTemp("", "entire-opencode-export-*.json")
   243→	if err != nil {
   244→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to create temp file: %w", err)
   245→	}
   246→	defer os.Remove(tmpFile.Name())
   247→
   248→	if _, err := tmpFile.Write(exportData); err != nil {
   249→		_ = tmpFile.Close()
   250→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to write export data: %w", err)
   251→	}
   252→	if err := tmpFile.Close(); err != nil {
   253→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to close temp file: %w", err)
   254→	}
   255→
   256→	return runOpenCodeImport(tmpFile.Name())
   257→}
   258→
   259→func (a *OpenCodeAgent) FormatResumeCommand(sessionID string) string {
   260→	if strings.TrimSpace(sessionID) == "" {
   261→		return "opencode"
   262→	}
   263→	return "opencode -s " + sessionID
   264→}
   265→
   266→// nonAlphanumericRegex matches any non-alphanumeric character.
   267→var nonAlphanumericRegex = regexp.MustCompile(`[^a-zA-Z0-9]`)
   268→
   269→// SanitizePathForOpenCode converts a path to a safe directory name.
   270→// Replaces any non-alphanumeric character with a dash (same approach as Claude/Gemini).
   271→func SanitizePathForOpenCode(path string) string {
   272→	return nonAlphanumericRegex.ReplaceAllString(path, "-")
   273→}
   274→
```

     1→package opencode
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"context"
     5→	"errors"
     6→	"fmt"
     7→	"os/exec"
     8→	"strings"
     9→	"time"
    10→)
    11→
    12→// openCodeCommandTimeout is the maximum time to wait for opencode CLI commands.
    13→const openCodeCommandTimeout = 30 * time.Second
    14→
    15→// runOpenCodeExport runs `opencode export <sessionID>` to export a session
    16→// from OpenCode's database. Returns the JSON export data as bytes.
    17→func runOpenCodeExport(sessionID string) ([]byte, error) {
    18→	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), openCodeCommandTimeout)
    19→	defer cancel()
    20→
    21→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "opencode", "export", sessionID)
    22→	output, err := cmd.Output()
    23→	if err != nil {
    24→		if ctx.Err() == context.DeadlineExceeded {
    25→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("opencode export timed out after %s", openCodeCommandTimeout)
    26→		}
    27→		// Get stderr for better error message
    28→		exitErr := &exec.ExitError{}
    29→		if errors.As(err, &exitErr) {
    30→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("opencode export failed: %w (stderr: %s)", err, string(exitErr.Stderr))
    31→		}
    32→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("opencode export failed: %w", err)
    33→	}
    34→
    35→	return output, nil
    36→}
    37→
    38→// runOpenCodeSessionDelete runs `opencode session delete <sessionID>` to remove
    39→// a session from OpenCode's database. Returns nil on success or if the session
    40→// doesn't exist (nothing to delete).
    41→func runOpenCodeSessionDelete(sessionID string) error {
    42→	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), openCodeCommandTimeout)
    43→	defer cancel()
    44→
    45→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "opencode", "session", "delete", sessionID)
    46→	if output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
    47→		if ctx.Err() == context.DeadlineExceeded {
    48→			return fmt.Errorf("opencode session delete timed out after %s", openCodeCommandTimeout)
    49→		}
    50→		// "Session not found" means the session doesn't exist — nothing to delete.
    51→		if strings.Contains(strings.ToLower(string(output)), "session not found") {
    52→			return nil
    53→		}
    54→		return fmt.Errorf("opencode session delete failed: %w (output: %s)", err, string(output))
    55→	}
    56→
    57→	return nil
    58→}
    59→
    60→// runOpenCodeImport runs `opencode import <file>` to import a session into
    61→// OpenCode's database. The import preserves the original session ID
    62→// from the export file.
    63→func runOpenCodeImport(exportFilePath string) error {
    64→	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), openCodeCommandTimeout)
    65→	defer cancel()
    66→
    67→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "opencode", "import", exportFilePath)
    68→	if output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
    69→		if ctx.Err() == context.DeadlineExceeded {
    70→			return fmt.Errorf("opencode import timed out after %s", openCodeCommandTimeout)
    71→		}
    72→		return fmt.Errorf("opencode import failed: %w (output: %s)", err, string(output))
    73→	}
    74→
    75→	return nil
    76→}
    77→

     1→package opencode
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"fmt"
     5→	"os"
     6→	"path/filepath"
     7→	"strings"
     8→
     9→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent"
    10→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/paths"
    11→)
    12→
    13→// Compile-time interface assertion
    14→var _ agent.HookSupport = (*OpenCodeAgent)(nil)
    15→
    16→const (
    17→	// pluginFileName is the name of the plugin file written to .opencode/plugins/
    18→	pluginFileName = "entire.ts"
    19→
    20→	// pluginDirName is the directory under .opencode/ where plugins live
    21→	pluginDirName = "plugins"
    22→
    23→	// entireMarker is a string present in the plugin file to identify it as Entire's
    24→	entireMarker = "Auto-generated by `entire enable --agent opencode`"
    25→)
    26→
    27→// getPluginPath returns the absolute path to the plugin file.
    28→func getPluginPath() (string, error) {
    29→	repoRoot, err := paths.RepoRoot()
    30→	if err != nil {
    31→		// Fallback to CWD if not in a git repo (e.g., during tests)
    32→		//nolint:forbidigo // Intentional fallback when RepoRoot() fails (tests run outside git repos)
    33→		repoRoot, err = os.Getwd()
    34→		if err != nil {
    35→			return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to get current directory: %w", err)
    36→		}
    37→	}
    38→	return filepath.Join(repoRoot, ".opencode", pluginDirName, pluginFileName), nil
    39→}
    40→
    41→// InstallHooks writes the Entire plugin file to .opencode/plugins/entire.ts.
    42→// Returns 1 if the plugin was installed, 0 if already present (idempotent).
    43→func (a *OpenCodeAgent) InstallHooks(localDev bool, force bool) (int, error) {
    44→	pluginPath, err := getPluginPath()
    45→	if err != nil {
    46→		return 0, err
    47→	}
    48→
    49→	// Check if already installed (idempotent) unless force
    50→	if !force {
    51→		if _, err := os.Stat(pluginPath); err == nil {
    52→			data, readErr := os.ReadFile(pluginPath) //nolint:gosec // Path constructed from repo root
    53→			if readErr == nil && strings.Contains(string(data), entireMarker) {
    54→				return 0, nil // Already installed
    55→			}
    56→		}
    57→	}
    58→
    59→	// Build the command prefix
    60→	var cmdPrefix string
    61→	if localDev {
    62→		cmdPrefix = "go run ${OPENCODE_PROJECT_DIR}/cmd/entire/main.go"
    63→	} else {
    64→		cmdPrefix = "entire"
    65→	}
    66→
    67→	// Generate plugin content from template
    68→	content := strings.ReplaceAll(pluginTemplate, entireCmdPlaceholder, cmdPrefix)
    69→
    70→	// Ensure directory exists
    71→	pluginDir := filepath.Dir(pluginPath)
    72→	//nolint:gosec // G301: Plugin directory needs standard permissions
    73→	if err := os.MkdirAll(pluginDir, 0o755); err != nil {
    74→		return 0, fmt.Errorf("failed to create plugin directory: %w", err)
    75→	}
    76→
    77→	// Write plugin file
    78→	//nolint:gosec // G306: Plugin file needs standard permissions for OpenCode to read
    79→	if err := os.WriteFile(pluginPath, []byte(content), 0o644); err != nil {
    80→		return 0, fmt.Errorf("failed to write plugin file: %w", err)
    81→	}
    82→
    83→	return 1, nil
    84→}
    85→
    86→// UninstallHooks removes the Entire plugin file.
    87→func (a *OpenCodeAgent) UninstallHooks() error {
    88→	pluginPath, err := getPluginPath()
    89→	if err != nil {
    90→		return err
    91→	}
    92→
    93→	if err := os.Remove(pluginPath); err != nil && !os.IsNotExist(err) {
    94→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to remove plugin file: %w", err)
    95→	}
    96→
    97→	return nil
    98→}
    99→
   100→// AreHooksInstalled checks if the Entire plugin file exists and contains the marker.
   101→func (a *OpenCodeAgent) AreHooksInstalled() bool {
   102→	pluginPath, err := getPluginPath()
   103→	if err != nil {
   104→		return false
   105→	}
   106→
   107→	data, err := os.ReadFile(pluginPath) //nolint:gosec // Path constructed from repo root
   108→	if err != nil {
   109→		return false
   110→	}
   111→
   112→	return strings.Contains(string(data), entireMarker)
   113→}
   114→
   115→// GetSupportedHooks returns the normalized lifecycle events this agent supports.
   116→// OpenCode's native hooks map to standard agent lifecycle events:
   117→//   - session-start → HookSessionStart
   118→//   - session-end   → HookSessionEnd
   119→//   - turn-start    → HookUserPromptSubmit (user prompt triggers a turn)
   120→//   - turn-end      → HookStop (agent response complete)
   121→//
   122→// Note: HookNames() returns 5 hooks (including "compaction"), but GetSupportedHooks()
   123→// returns only 4. The "compaction" hook is OpenCode-specific with no standard HookType
   124→// mapping — it is handled via ParseHookEvent but not advertised as a standard lifecycle event.
   125→func (a *OpenCodeAgent) GetSupportedHooks() []agent.HookType {
   126→	return []agent.HookType{
   127→		agent.HookSessionStart,
   128→		agent.HookSessionEnd,
   129→		agent.HookUserPromptSubmit,
   130→		agent.HookStop,
   131→	}
   132→}
   133→

Based on the search results, here's information about OpenCode CLI's non-interactive mode and prompt flag:

## Non-Interactive Mode

You can run OpenCode in non-interactive mode by passing a prompt directly, which is useful for scripting, automation, or when you want a quick answer without launching the full TUI. You can run OpenCode in non-interactive mode by passing a prompt directly as a command-line argument, which is useful for scripting, automation, or when you want a quick answer without launching the full TUI.

## How It Works

In this mode, OpenCode will process your prompt, print the result to standard output, and then exit. All permissions are auto-approved for the session.

## Related Flags

For non-interactive mode usage:

- When using non-interactive mode with opencode run, use the --title flag to set a custom name for the session, which requires a message argument and is designed for scripting and automation.

- You can disable the spinner animation with the -q or --quiet flag, which is particularly useful when running OpenCode from scripts or automated workflows.

- You can control which tools the AI assistant has access to in non-interactive mode using --allowedTools (comma-separated list of tools that the agent is allowed to use) or --excludedTools (comma-separated list of tools that the agent is not allowed to use).


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.

# OpenCode CLI Flags for Non-Interactive Execution

## Running Non-Interactively

The `run` command executes OpenCode without the interactive TUI:

```bash
opencode run "Your prompt here"
```

## Key Flags for Non-Interactive Mode

**Model Selection:**
- `--model` or `-m`: Specify model as `provider/model` (e.g., `anthropic/claude-3-5-sonnet`)

**Session Management:**
- `--continue` or `-c`: Resume the last session
- `--session` or `-s`: Continue a specific session ID
- `--fork`: Branch a session when continuing

**Agent & Configuration:**
- `--agent`: Select a specific agent to use
- `--file` or `-f`: Attach file(s) to your message

**Output Control:**
- `--format`: Choose between `default` (formatted) or `json` (raw JSON events)

**Additional Options:**
- `--title`: Set a custom session title
- `--attach`: Connect to a running server (e.g., `http://localhost:4096`)
- `--share`: Make the session shareable

## Important Note

The documentation provided doesn't explicitly mention flags for "allowed tools" or traditional "quiet mode." Tool configuration happens through the config file, not CLI flags during execution.
```

# OpenCode CLI Flags Analysis

Based on the README documentation provided, here are the available CLI flags for OpenCode:

## Command-Line Flags

| Flag | Short | Purpose |
|------|-------|---------|
| `--help` | `-h` | Display help information |
| `--debug` | `-d` | Enable debug mode |
| `--cwd` | `-c` | Set current working directory |
| `--prompt` | `-p` | "Run a single prompt in non-interactive mode" |
| `--output-format` | `-f` | Output format for non-interactive mode (text, json) |
| `--quiet` | `-q` | "Hide spinner in non-interactive mode" |

## Key Observations

The documentation does not mention:
- A dedicated `opencode run` subcommand
- Tool control flags like `allowedTools` or `excludedTools`
- Model selection flags beyond the interactive `Ctrl+O` model dialog

## Non-Interactive Mode

The primary non-interactive execution uses the `-p` flag with optional `-f` for output formatting and `-q` to suppress visual feedback. The documentation states: "In this mode, OpenCode will process your prompt, print the result to standard output, and then exit."

Note: The repository was archived on September 18, 2025, with development continuing under the project name "Crush."

# OpenCode CLI Commands and Flags

## Main Commands

**`opencode run`** - Execute prompts non-interactively:
- "Run opencode in non-interactive mode by passing a prompt directly"
- Usage: `opencode run [message..]`
- Example: `opencode run "Explain the use of context in Go"`

### Run Command Flags

| Flag | Short | Purpose |
|------|-------|---------|
| `--command` | | Specify the command to execute |
| `--continue` | `-c` | Resume the last session |
| `--session` | `-s` | Continue a specific session ID |
| `--fork` | | Create a branch of an existing session |
| `--share` | | Share the session |
| `--model` | `-m` | Select model as `provider/model` |
| `--agent` | | Choose an agent |
| `--file` | `-f` | Attach file(s) |
| `--format` | | Output as `default` or `json` |
| `--title` | | Set session title |
| `--attach` | | Connect to running server |
| `--port` | | Local server port |

## Other Key Commands

- **`opencode serve`** - Start headless API server
- **`opencode web`** - Launch web interface
- **`opencode attach`** - Connect TUI to remote backend
- **`opencode models`** - List available models
- **`opencode auth`** - Manage credentials
- **`opencode session`** - Manage sessions
- **`opencode stats`** - View usage statistics

## Global Flags

- `--help` / `-h` - Display help
- `--version` / `-v` - Show version
- `--log-level` - Set verbosity (DEBUG, INFO, WARN, ERROR)

No explicit "quiet" or "silent" flags appear in the documentation.

> AGENT

Everything looks good. Here's what I added:

**`cmd/entire/cli/e2e_test/agent_runner.go`:**
- Added `AgentNameOpenCode = "opencode"` constant
- Added `opencode` case to the `NewAgentRunner` factory
- Added `OpenCodeRunner` struct with `NewOpenCodeRunner`, `IsAvailable`, `RunPrompt`, and `RunPromptWithTools`
  - Uses `opencode run "<prompt>"` for non-interactive execution (all permissions auto-approved)
  - Model configurable via `E2E_OPENCODE_MODEL` env var or `--model` flag (no default — uses project config)
  - Timeout via `E2E_TIMEOUT` env var (default 2m), same as other runners
  - `tools` parameter is accepted but ignored (OpenCode's `run` auto-approves all tools)

**`mise.toml`:**
- Added `test:e2e:opencode` task: `E2E_AGENT=opencode go test -tags=e2e -count=1 -timeout=30m -v ./cmd/entire/cli/e2e_test/...`

You can now run:
```bash
mise run test:e2e:opencode
# Or with a specific model:
REDACTED mise run test:e2e:opencode
```

The existing E2E test scenarios (basic workflow, checkpoints, rewind, etc.) will all run against OpenCode — no separate test files needed since the tests are agent-agnostic. The subagent test (`scenario_subagent_test.go`) will be skipped since it's Claude-specific.

    72→
    73→	env := NewTestEnv(t)

    82→	env.RunEntireEnable(strategyName)
    83→
    84→	// Commit all files created by `entire enable` so they survive git stash -u operations.
    85→	// Without this, stash operations would stash away the hooks config and entire settings,
    86→	// causing hooks to not fire for subsequent prompts and stash pop conflicts.
    87→	// Using "git add ." is safe in test repos since we control all content.
    88→	env.GitAddAll()

     1→// Entire CLI plugin for OpenCode
     2→// Auto-generated by `entire enable --agent opencode`
     3→// Do not edit manually — changes will be overwritten on next install.
     4→// Requires Bun runtime (used by OpenCode's plugin system for loading ESM plugins).
     5→import type { Plugin } from "@opencode-ai/plugin"
     6→
     7→export const EntirePlugin: Plugin = async ({ $, directory }) => {
     8→  const ENTIRE_CMD = "__ENTIRE_CMD__"
     9→  // Track seen user messages to fire turn-start only once per message
    10→  const seenUserMessages = new Set<string>()
    11→  // Track current session ID for message events (which don't include sessionID)
    12→  let currentSessionID: string | null = null
    13→  // In-memory store for message metadata (role, tokens, etc.)
    14→  const messageStore = new Map<string, any>()
    15→
    16→  /**
    17→   * Pipe JSON payload to an entire hooks command.
    18→   * Errors are logged but never thrown — plugin failures must not crash OpenCode.
    19→   */
    20→  async function callHook(hookName: string, payload: Record<string, unknown>) {
    21→    try {
    22→      const json = JSON.stringify(payload)
    23→      await $`echo ${json} | ${ENTIRE_CMD} hooks opencode ${hookName}`.quiet().nothrow()
    24→    } catch {
    25→      // Silently ignore — plugin failures must not crash OpenCode
    26→    }
    27→  }
    28→
    29→  return {
    30→    event: async ({ event }) => {
    31→      switch (event.type) {
    32→        case "session.created": {
    33→          const session = (event as any).properties?.info
    34→          if (!session?.id) break
    35→          // Reset per-session tracking state when switching sessions.
    36→          if (currentSessionID !== session.id) {
    37→            seenUserMessages.clear()
    38→            messageStore.clear()
    39→          }
    40→          currentSessionID = session.id
    41→          await callHook("session-start", {
    42→            session_id: session.id,
    43→          })
    44→          break
    45→        }
    46→
    47→        case "message.updated": {
    48→          const msg = (event as any).properties?.info
    49→          if (!msg) break
    50→          // Store message metadata (role, time, tokens, etc.)
    51→          messageStore.set(msg.id, msg)
    52→          break
    53→        }
    54→
    55→        case "message.part.updated": {
    56→          const part = (event as any).properties?.part
    57→          if (!part?.messageID) break
    58→
    59→          // Fire turn-start on the first text part of a new user message
    60→          const msg = messageStore.get(part.messageID)
    61→          if (msg?.role === "user" && part.type === "text" && !seenUserMessages.has(msg.id)) {
    62→            seenUserMessages.add(msg.id)
    63→            const sessionID = msg.sessionID ?? currentSessionID
    64→            if (sessionID) {
    65→              await callHook("turn-start", {
    66→                session_id: sessionID,
    67→                prompt: part.text ?? "",
    68→              })
    69→            }
    70→          }
    71→          break
    72→        }
    73→
    74→        case "session.idle": {
    75→          const sessionID = (event as any).properties?.sessionID
    76→          if (!sessionID) break
    77→          // Go hook handler will call `opencode export` to get the transcript
    78→          await callHook("turn-end", {
    79→            session_id: sessionID,
    80→          })
    81→          break
    82→        }
    83→
    84→        case "session.compacted": {
    85→          const sessionID = (event as any).properties?.sessionID
    86→          if (!sessionID) break
    87→          await callHook("compaction", {
    88→            session_id: sessionID,
    89→          })
    90→          break
    91→        }
    92→
    93→        case "session.deleted": {
    94→          const session = (event as any).properties?.info
    95→          if (!session?.id) break
    96→          seenUserMessages.clear()
    97→          messageStore.clear()
    98→          currentSessionID = null
    99→          await callHook("session-end", {
   100→            session_id: session.id,
   101→          })
   102→          break
   103→        }
   104→      }
   105→    },
   106→  }
   107→}
   108→

> AGENT

Consistent pattern. Every test fails because **OpenCode's `run` command doesn't fire plugin events** (or the plugin doesn't load in non-interactive mode). Here's the summary:

**Root Cause: All tests fail for the same reason.**

When `opencode run "prompt"` executes in non-interactive mode, the **plugin system doesn't fire events** (`session.created`, `message.part.updated`, `session.idle`, etc.). This means:

1. The Entire plugin (`.opencode/plugins/entire.ts`) never calls `entire hooks opencode <hook-name>`
2. No session-start, turn-start, turn-end, or session-end hooks fire
3. No checkpoints or rewind points are created
4. The agent creates files successfully, but all checkpoint/rewind/trailer assertions fail

This is fundamentally different from Claude Code and Gemini CLI where the `-p` non-interactive mode **does** fire lifecycle hooks. The test infrastructure (agent creates file -> hooks create checkpoints -> test verifies checkpoints) requires hooks to fire during agent execution.

**What's needed to fix this:**

There are a few possible approaches:
1. **Check if newer OpenCode versions support plugins in `run` mode** - the plugin events may simply not be wired up for non-interactive execution
2. **Use `opencode run` with `--format json`** and post-process events manually to simulate hook calls
3. **Wrap `opencode run` in the E2E runner** so it manually calls `entire hooks opencode session-start` before and `entire hooks opencode turn-end` / `session-end` after the run completes (simulating the hook lifecycle)
4. **Skip OpenCode E2E tests** with a clear message that plugin hooks aren't supported in non-interactive mode

Want me to investigate option 3 (wrapping with manual hook calls) or take a different approach?

title:	Unable to use opencode cli in a non-interactive pipeline
state:	OPEN
author:	S0LERA
labels:	bug
comments:	1
assignees:	thdxr
projects:	
milestone:	
number:	13851
--
### Description

### Summary
In opencode v1.1.60 (running inside an OpenShift/Kubernetes pod), I cannot achieve a fully non-interactive, general-purpose automation workflow where:

- `opencode run "Do X"` creates/edits/deletes files in the working repository using tools like write/edit without any human interaction for arbitrary repositories and tasks (CI-style automation driven from Python via subprocess)
 
Despite tools being available, the agent does not execute write and the session ends in cancel. The alternative “server + attach” approach also lacks a batch mechanism, and run --attach fails.
 
### Expected behavior
Running a command like:
 
`opencode run "Implement X, create/modify/delete files"`
 
should:
- use tools (write/edit)
- apply real filesystem changes in the repo
- complete non-interactively (CI-friendly)
- work generally across repositories/tasks
 
### Actual behavior (observed facts)
 
#### 1) Sessions are created with a restrictive permission preset
Every opencode run execution creates the session with an initial permission set that includes:
- question: deny
- plan_enter: deny
- plan_exit: deny
 
Example from logs:
```
  service=session ... created permission=[
    {"permission":"question","pattern":"*","action":"deny"},
    {"permission":"plan_enter","pattern":"*","action":"deny"},
    {"permission":"plan_exit","pattern":"*","action":"deny"}
  ]
```
 
#### 2) Tools exist but write is never executed
The tool registry shows tools such as write, edit, bash, etc. are available, but in practice the agent:
- starts the loop
- may execute something trivial (e.g. bash ls)
- ends with cancel
- does not execute write
- therefore no files are created/modified/deleted
 
### Why configuration does not fix it
 
#### A) opencode.jsonc permissive permissions do not override the session preset
Even with permissive configuration such as:
```

title:	Feature request: let plugins detect if they are in an interactive session
state:	OPEN
author:	ariane-emory
labels:	
comments:	0
assignees:	thdxr
projects:	
milestone:	
number:	3201
--
Please provide some function by which a plugin can detect on whether it is running in an interactive session (as opposed to an API call or `opencode run` invocation).

### Motivation: 

I have a plugin to ring a bell when session.idle, to alert me when work has been completed.

I also have custom slash commands that invoke `opencode run` to do prompt enhancement.

Presently, using one of these commands results in the bell ringing twice: once when the nested `opencode run` command completes, and then a second time when the work in response to the prompt I had input in the interactive session where I ran the command has finished. The earlier beep is not desired: the intent of the bell plugin was to alert me when the work is finished, not when the prompt enhancement that the nested `opencode run` invocation performs has complete (after which the enhanced prompt is handed back to the interactive opencode session and the 'work' proper begins

Some function like `AmIRunningInAnInteractiveSession()` that I could use in the plugin would suffice to accomplish my goal.
```

> AGENT

> DEVELOPER

quick question: did we run entire enable --agent opencode at the beginning of the tests so the right hooks are setup?

> AGENT

Yes — `NewFeatureBranchEnv` calls `RunEntireEnable(strategyName)` which runs:

```
entire enable --agent opencode --strategy manual-commit --telemetry=false --force
```

And the test output confirms it works:

```
Agent: OpenCode

Installed 1 hooks for OpenCode - AI-powered terminal coding agent (Preview)
✓ Project configured (.entire/settings.json)
✓ Created orphan branch 'entire/checkpoints/v1' for session metadata

Ready.
```

So the `.opencode/plugins/entire.ts` plugin file is installed. The issue is that when `opencode run "prompt"` executes, the plugin's `callHook()` runs `entire hooks opencode <hook-name>` — but from issue #3201, we know plugins **do** fire in `run` mode. So the problem might be something else, like:

- The `entire` binary not being found by the plugin's shell command
- The plugin's `$` (Bun shell) not inheriting the test's `PATH`
- Or the hook command failing silently (the plugin swallows all errors with `catch {}`)

Want me to add some debug logging to the test runner to check if the hooks are actually being invoked (e.g., check `.entire/logs/` after a run)?

> DEVELOPER

yes

> AGENT

Found it. The key lines are:

```
permission requested: external_directory (/private/var/folders/gz/.../T/TestE2E_DebugOpenCodeHooks.../001/*); auto-rejecting
✗ write failed
Error: The user rejected permission to use this specific tool call.
```

**The hooks ARE firing** (session-start and turn-start appear in the logs). But **OpenCode is rejecting the write tool** because the temp directory is outside the project's allowed paths. It's treating it as an "external directory" and auto-rejecting the permission.

Also note: the hooks only logged `session-start` and `turn-start` — no `turn-end` or `session-end`, which means the session never properly completed (the agent errored out on the permission rejection before finishing its work).

The fix is to configure OpenCode to allow writing in the test directory. This likely needs an `opencode.json` config in the test repo. Let me check what config options control this.

# OpenCode Permissions Configuration

Based on the documentation provided, the permissions configuration options are limited. Here's what's available:

## Current Permission Options

The config supports a `permission` option where you can set tool-level approval requirements:

```json
{
  "permission": {
    "edit": "ask",
    "bash": "ask"
  }
}
```

The documentation states: *"By default, opencode **allows all operations** without requiring explicit approval. You can change this using the `permission` option."*

This shows you can configure individual tools to require user approval by setting them to `"ask"`.

## Limitations in Documented Features

The provided documentation does **not** cover:

- Configuration for external directory write permissions
- Auto-approval settings for non-interactive or CLI run modes
- Granular permission scoping by directory or file type
- Bypassing approval in automated environments

The documentation only demonstrates requiring approval for specific tools like `edit` and `bash`, but doesn't detail alternative permission states (like auto-allow vs. ask) or mode-specific configurations.

For complete permission customization options, particularly for automation scenarios, you may need to consult additional documentation or the project's configuration schema directly.
```

#### 2) opencode attach is not batch/non-interactive
`opencode attach --help` only supports:
- --dir
- --session
- --password
 
It does not provide:
- --message / --prompt
- --agent
- --model
- “run once and exit” behavior
 
Therefore, even if opencode serve is running, there is no CLI way to “send a prompt and apply changes” non-interactively.
 
#### 3) opencode session and opencode acp do not provide a CLI batch execution path
- opencode session only supports list
- opencode acp only starts an ACP server (no CLI client command to send tasks/apply changes)
 
### Impact on Python/CI automation
If automation relies on:
 
`subprocess.run(["opencode", "run", ...])`
 
it cannot reliably apply repo changes because:
- the session preset blocks the internal flow needed to reach write/edit
- there is no alternative CLI batch mode for serve
- run --attach fails with No context found for instance
 
### Environment
- opencode version: 1.1.60
- platform: OpenShift/Kubernetes pod
- working directory example: /tmp/workdir/TEST
- model: gpt-5.1-codex-mini
- goal: fully non-interactive, general automation for arbitrary repositories/tasks

 
### Requested improvements / questions
1) Provide a supported non-interactive mode for opencode run that can still execute repo-changing tools (write/edit) without requiring question/plan_*, or make the session permission preset configurable.
2) Fix opencode run --attach (No context found for instance) or document required steps to initialize context.
3) Add a batch mode to opencode attach (e.g., --message/--prompt, --agent, --model, “send and exit”) to enable CI automation against opencode serve without implementing a custom HTTP client.

### Plugins

_No response_

### OpenCode version

1.1.60

### Steps to reproduce

1) In an empty working directory:
 
`opencode run --print-logs --log-level DEBUG -m litellm/azure-codex "Create a file app.py"`
 
2) Result:
- app.py is not created
- logs show session created with question/plan_* deny
- no write execution, session ends in cancel
 
3) Server attempt:
`opencode serve --hostname 127.0.0.1 --port 4096`
`opencode run --attach http://127.0.0.1:4096/ --print-logs --log-level DEBUG --agent autoaccept -m litellm/azure-codex "Create a file app.py"`
 
Result: fails with No context found for instance
 
4) Attach help confirms no batch mode:
`opencode attach --help`

_No response_

debian

bash

# OpenCode Permissions Overview

## Permission Types

OpenCode supports these permission categories:

- **File operations**: `read`, `edit`, `glob`, `grep`, `list`
- **Execution**: `bash`, `task`, `skill`
- **Web**: `webfetch`, `websearch`, `codesearch`
- **System**: `lsp`, `todoread`, `todowrite`, `external_directory`, `doom_loop`

Each resolves to `"allow"`, `"ask"`, or `"deny"`.

## Configuring external_directory

The `external_directory` permission allows tool access to paths outside your project workspace. Usage pattern:

```json
{
  "permission": {
    "external_directory": {
      "~/projects/personal/**": "allow"
    }
  }
}
```

You can combine it with tool-specific restrictions. For example, to allow reads but block edits in external paths:

```json
{
  "permission": {
    "external_directory": {"~/projects/personal/**": "allow"},
    "edit": {"~/projects/personal/**": "deny"}
  }
}
```

## Non-Interactive/Run Mode Auto-Approval

The documentation doesn't address automatic approval in non-interactive modes. To enable automatic execution, use `"allow"` for relevant permissions in your config.

## Allowing Specific Directories

Use granular rules with path patterns:

```json
{
  "permission": {
    "edit": {
      "*": "deny",
      "packages/web/src/content/docs/*.mdx": "allow"
    }
  }
}
```

Patterns support `*` (multiple chars) and `?` (single char) wildcards, plus `~` or `$HOME` expansion.
```

    72→

    77→	env.GitCommit("Initial commit")
    78→	env.GitCheckoutNewBranch("feature/e2e-test")
    79→
    80→	// Use `entire enable` to set up everything (hooks, settings, etc.)
    81→	// This sets up .entire/settings.json and .claude/settings.json with hooks
    82→	env.RunEntireEnable(strategyName)
    83→
    84→	// Commit all files created by `entire enable` so they survive git stash -u operations.
    85→	// Without this, stash operations would stash away the hooks config and entire settings,
    86→	// causing hooks to not fire for subsequent prompts and stash pop conflicts.
    87→	// Using "git add ." is safe in test repos since we control all content.
    88→	env.GitAddAll()
    89→	env.GitCommit("Add entire and agent config")
    90→
    91→	return env
    92→}

> DEVELOPER

wait: why are we writing to the tmp folder out side? or what is?

> AGENT

Good question. Let me re-read the error:

```
permission requested: external_directory (/private/var/folders/gz/...REDACTED*); auto-rejecting
```

The test repo itself IS in a temp dir (`t.TempDir()`). OpenCode's working directory is set to that temp dir (via `cmd.Dir = workDir`). But OpenCode considers it an "external directory" because `/private/var/folders/.../T/...` doesn't look like a normal project path to it — it's outside the user's home directory project structure.

The issue is that OpenCode determines the "project root" on startup (likely by looking for git root or config files), and if the resolved path is in `/var/folders/...` (macOS temp), it may not recognize it as the project directory, or it requires explicit permission for any paths in that location.

Let me confirm by checking exactly what OpenCode considers the project directory vs what it's rejecting:

The error says `external_directory (/private/var/folders/.../001/*)` — that's the **same directory** as the repo root. So OpenCode is treating its own working directory as "external." This is likely a known OpenCode bug with temp dirs / symlinked paths (macOS `/var` -> `/private/var`).

The `opencode.json` permission config I added should fix this — it tells OpenCode to allow external directory access. Want me to run the debug test to verify?

    15→// TestMain builds the CLI binary once and checks agent availability before running tests.
    16→func TestMain(m *testing.M) {
    17→	// Determine which agent to test with
    18→	defaultAgent = os.Getenv("E2E_AGENT")
    19→	if defaultAgent == "" {
    20→		defaultAgent = AgentNameClaudeCode
    21→	}
    22→
    23→	// Check if the agent is available
    24→	runner := NewAgentRunner(defaultAgent, AgentRunnerConfig{})
    25→	available, err := runner.IsAvailable()
    26→	if !available {
    27→		fmt.Printf("Agent %s not available (%v), skipping E2E tests\n", defaultAgent, err)
    28→		// Exit 0 to not fail CI when agent isn't configured
    29→		os.Exit(0)
    30→	}
    31→
    32→	// Build binary once to a temp directory
    33→	tmpDir, err := os.MkdirTemp("", "entire-e2e-test-*")
    34→	if err != nil {
    35→		fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "failed to create temp dir for binary: %v\n", err)
    36→		os.Exit(1)
    37→	}
    38→
    39→	testBinaryPath = filepath.Join(tmpDir, "entire")
    40→
    41→	moduleRoot := findModuleRoot()
    42→	ctx := context.Background()
    43→
    44→	buildCmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "go", "build", "-o", testBinaryPath, ".")
    45→	buildCmd.Dir = filepath.Join(moduleRoot, "cmd", "entire")
    46→
    47→	buildOutput, err := buildCmd.CombinedOutput()
    48→	if err != nil {
    49→		fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "failed to build CLI binary: %v\nOutput: %s\n", err, buildOutput)
    50→		os.RemoveAll(tmpDir)
    51→		os.Exit(1)
    52→	}
    53→
    54→	// Add binary to PATH so hooks can find it.
    55→	// This is safe because:
    56→	// 1. TestMain runs once before any tests (no parallel conflict with os.Setenv)
    57→	// 2. Each test package gets its own TestMain execution
    58→	// 3. PATH is restored in the same TestMain after m.Run() completes
    59→	// 4. The binary path is unique per test run (temp dir)
    60→	origPath := os.Getenv("PATH")
    61→	os.Setenv("PATH", tmpDir+string(os.PathListSeparator)+origPath)
    62→
    63→	// Run tests
    64→	code := m.Run()
    65→
    66→	// Cleanup
    67→	os.Setenv("PATH", origPath)
    68→	os.RemoveAll(tmpDir)
    69→	os.Exit(code)
    70→}
    71→
    72→// findModuleRoot finds the Go module root by walking up from the current file.
    73→func findModuleRoot() string {
    74→	_, thisFile, _, ok := runtime.Caller(0)
    75→	if !ok {
    76→		panic("failed to get current file path via runtime.Caller")
    77→	}
    78→	dir := filepath.Dir(thisFile)
    79→
    80→	for {
    81→		if _, err := os.Stat(filepath.Join(dir, "go.mod")); err == nil {
    82→			return dir
    83→		}
    84→		parent := filepath.Dir(dir)
    85→		if parent == dir {
    86→			panic("could not find go.mod starting from " + thisFile)
    87→		}
    88→		dir = parent
    89→	}
    90→}
    91→

> AGENT

Still getting `external_directory` rejection, even with the path inside the repo. The issue is that `.tmp-e2e/` is inside the **parent** git repo (cli_experiments), but the test creates a **new** git repo inside it. OpenCode probably determines the project root from the inner `.git`, and then considers the parent path structure.

Actually, looking more carefully at the error:
```
permission requested: external_directory (/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli_experiments/.REDACTED*); auto-rejecting
```

It's rejecting the **test repo's own directory** as external. This suggests `opencode run` in non-interactive mode auto-rejects ALL `external_directory` permissions regardless of path. Let me verify this hypothesis by checking the issue we found earlier:

> AGENT

Same error even inside the repo. The path is the **test repo's own directory** being rejected:

```
permission requested: external_directory (/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli_experiments/.REDACTED*); auto-rejecting
```

This is a known OpenCode bug/limitation: in `run` (non-interactive) mode, `external_directory` permission defaults to `"ask"`, which becomes auto-reject since there's no user to prompt. It happens even when the path **is** the project directory itself.

The location doesn't matter - we could put the repo in `~/projects/` and it would still happen. The `opencode.json` `external_directory: allow` config isn't hiding a real issue; it's working around OpenCode's `run` mode treating all directory permission requests as unanswerable.

Want me to:
1. Add the `opencode.json` with just `"external_directory": {"*": "allow"}` (since `run` mode already auto-approves everything else anyway)
2. Revert the `.tmp-e2e` approach and go back to `t.TempDir()`

Or do you want to take a different approach?

> DEVELOPER

could we add only the current tmp directory (and yes go back to t.TempDir()) top opencode.json ath the beginning? would that work instead of `*`

> AGENT

Good idea — we know the `RepoDir` at setup time so we can scope it exactly. Let me revert the `.tmp-e2e` approach and add a scoped `opencode.json` in `NewFeatureBranchEnv`.

# OpenCode Configuration File Placement

OpenCode supports both **JSON and JSONC formats**. The configuration follows a precedence order across multiple locations:

## File Names
The config file can be named either `opencode.json` or `opencode.jsonc` (which allows comments).

## Location Options

**Project Root** (Highest Priority for Standard Configs)
- Place `opencode.json` in your project root
- "When OpenCode starts up, it looks for a config file in the current directory or traverse up to the nearest Git directory"

**Global Configuration**
- Location: `~/.config/opencode/opencode.json`
- Used for user-wide preferences

**Custom Path**
- Set via `OPENCODE_CONFIG` environment variable for a specific file location

**Custom Directory**
- Set via `OPENCODE_CONFIG_DIR` environment variable to load agents, commands, and plugins

**Remote Configuration**
- Organizations can provide defaults via `.well-known/opencode` endpoint

## Precedence Order
Configurations are merged (not replaced), with later sources overriding conflicts:
1. Remote config
2. Global config
3. Custom config (env var)
4. Project config
5. `.opencode` directories
6. Inline config (env var)

The project root `opencode.json` has the highest priority among standard config files and is safe to commit to Git.
```
