```
#### 1) Sessions are created with a restrictive permission preset
Every opencode run execution creates the session with an initial permission set that includes:
- question: deny
- plan_enter: deny
- plan_exit: deny
 
Example from logs:
--
  service=session ... created permission=[
    {"permission":"question","pattern":"*","action":"deny"},
    {"permission":"plan_enter","pattern":"*","action":"deny"},
    {"permission":"plan_exit","pattern":"*","action":"deny"}
  ]
```
 
#### 2) Tools exist but write is never executed
The tool registry shows tools such as write, edit, bash, etc. are available, but in practice the agent:
--
### Why configuration does not fix it
 
#### A) opencode.jsonc permissive permissions do not override the session preset
Even with permissive configuration such as:
 
`"permission": { "*": "allow" }`
 
(and/or explicitly allowing question/plan_enter/plan_exit), the session still starts with question/plan_* = deny. This suggests a run/session-bootstrap preset taking precedence over config.
 
#### B) Built-in agents start with deny/ask rules (non-interactive hostile)
opencode agent list shows built-in agents (build, general, explore, etc.) include early rules like:
- question: deny
- plan_enter: deny
--
I created a custom agent allowing tools and permissions and ran:
 
`opencode run --agent autoaccept ...`
 
The agent is loaded (logs show > autoaccept · azure-codex), but sessions still start with question/plan_* deny and write is not executed.
 
--
1) Provide a supported non-interactive mode for opencode run that can still execute repo-changing tools (write/edit) without requiring question/plan_*, or make the session permission preset configurable.
2) Fix opencode run --attach (No context found for instance) or document required steps to initialize context.
3) Add a batch mode to opencode attach (e.g., --message/--prompt, --agent, --model, “send and exit”) to enable CI automation against opencode serve without implementing a custom HTTP client.

### Plugins
```

> AGENT

Key finding from the issue:

> **opencode.jsonc permissive permissions do not override the session preset**. Even with `"permission": { "*": "allow" }`, the session still starts with question/plan_* = deny. This suggests a **run/session-bootstrap preset taking precedence over config.**

So the `opencode.json` permission config is ignored for `run` mode — there's a hardcoded session preset that overrides it. This is a known, open bug (#13851).

However, the issue reporter mentions that **write/edit** work for some users. The problem in that issue was specifically about `question` and `plan_*` denial. Our problem is `external_directory`. Let me check the OpenCode version to see if there's been a fix:

# Creating Custom Agents with Permissions

Based on the documentation, here's how to create a custom agent with specific permissions:

## Markdown Format (Recommended)

Create a file at `~/.config/opencode/agents/your-agent.md`:

```yaml
---
description: Your agent's purpose
mode: subagent
permission:
  edit: ask
  bash:
    "git *": allow
    "*": deny
  webfetch: allow
---
Your system prompt instructions here.
```

## JSON Format

In your `opencode.json`:

```json
{
  "agent": {
    "your-agent": {
      "description": "Agent description",
      "mode": "subagent",
      "permission": {
        "edit": "ask",
        "bash": {
          "git status": "allow",
          "*": "deny"
        },
        "webfetch": "allow"
      }
    }
  }
}
```

## Permission Levels

According to the docs, permissions support three values: `"ask"` (prompt for approval), `"allow"` (no approval needed), and `"deny"` (disable tool). Currently, configurable permissions apply to `edit`, `bash`, and `webfetch` only.

**Note:** The documentation doesn't mention an `external_directory` permission option, so this may not be available through the current agent configuration system.
```

Now I can see exactly what's happening. The ruleset has our config rule:

```
{"permission":"external_directory","pattern":"/private/var/.../TestE2E_DebugOpenCodeHooks894881778/001/*","action":"allow"}
```

But the pattern being evaluated is:

```
/private/var/.../TestE2E_DebugClaude CodeHooks894881778/001/*
```

Notice: **`DebugClaude CodeHooks`** vs **`DebugOpenCodeHooks`**! There's a space in "Claude Code" that's mangling the test name. The permission pattern has the correct path, but the actual request has a different path with "Claude Code" embedded in the directory name.

This means the test name is being corrupted somewhere. Let me check:

> TOOL

> AGENT

The test name is fine. But let me look at the actual path more carefully in that log line:

```
pattern=/private/var/.../TestE2E_DebugClaude CodeHooks894881778/001/*
```

"Claude Code" with a space — that's not the test name, that's from somewhere else. The `t.TempDir()` directory name includes the test name, but the agent (OpenCode using Claude Haiku) might be resolving the path through its own agent type name. Let me look at where the permission request comes from — it seems like OpenCode is internally resolving the path and inserting "Claude Code" (its agent type?).

Wait, actually — look at the stderr from the test output earlier:
```
> build · claude-haiku-4-5
```

OpenCode is using Claude Haiku as its model. And the path corruption `TestE2E_DebugClaude CodeHooks` — that looks like a rendering bug where OpenCode's agent type "Claude Code" gets injected into the displayed path. The actual path on disk is `TestE2E_DebugOpenCodeHooks`, but OpenCode shows it as `TestE2E_DebugClaude CodeHooks` in the permission prompt.

So the **permission pattern is correct** and the **actual disk path is correct**, but OpenCode is evaluating against a **mangled pattern** with "Claude Code" injected. This is an OpenCode display/permission evaluation bug.

Let me verify — the ruleset evaluation line shows our allow rule IS in the list, but the `evaluated` line shows it matched the catch-all `ask` instead:

```
action={"permission":"external_directory","pattern":"*","action":"ask"} evaluated
```

The `*` catch-all `ask` rule is matching before our specific allow rule. This is likely because OpenCode evaluates rules in order and the catch-all `ask` comes before our specific `allow`. Let me revert the debug logging and just try a different config approach — maybe put the `external_directory` wildcard allow:

> TOOL

> AGENT

The issue is clear from the debug logs: our specific allow rule IS in the ruleset, but OpenCode evaluates the catch-all `"external_directory": {"*": "ask"}` rule first and it wins. The rule ordering means specific patterns can't override the catch-all. But from issue #9927's logs, I noticed that OpenCode also had `{"permission":"external_directory","action":"allow","pattern":"*"}` in some rulesets.

Let me try setting the `*` pattern to `allow` in our opencode.json — this directly overrides the catch-all:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01KgYaRXahrtzE7Fv9HarR1c
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli_experiments/cmd/entire/cli/e2e_test/testenv.go",
  "old_string": "\t// OpenCode's `run` (non-interactive) mode defaults external_directory permission\n\t// to \"ask\", which auto-rejects since there's no user to prompt. This happens even\n\t// when the path is the project directory itself. Scope the allow to this test repo only.\n\tif defaultAgent == AgentNameOpenCode {\n\t\tenv.WriteFile(\"opencode.json\", fmt.Sprintf(`{\n  \"permission\": {\n    \"external_directory\": {\n      \"%s/*\": \"allow\"\n    }\n  }\n}\n`, env.RepoDir))\n\t}",
  "new_string": "\t// OpenCode's `run` (non-interactive) mode defaults external_directory permission\n\t// to \"ask\", which auto-rejects since there's no user to prompt — even when the\n\t// path is the project directory itself. Override to \"allow\" for the test repo.\n\t// Note: specific path patterns don't work here because OpenCode evaluates the\n\t// catch-all \"ask\" rule before specific \"allow\" rules (known issue).\n\tif defaultAgent == AgentNameOpenCode {\n\t\tenv.WriteFile(\"opencode.json\", `{\n  \"permission\": {\n    \"external_directory\": \"allow\"\n  }\n}\n`)\n\t}"
}
```

The file write "succeeded" but to a **wrong path**:

```
← Write ../../TestE2E_DebugClaude CodeHooks3091417100/001/hello.go
```

It wrote using a relative path `../../` from wherever OpenCode thinks the project root is, and the path contains `Claude Code` with a space (the same mangling issue). The file likely ended up somewhere unexpected, not in the test repo.

This is an OpenCode bug where the model name ("Claude Code") leaks into path resolution. But the more immediate issue is that the file isn't being written to the correct directory. Let me check if `turn-end` fires now that the external_directory permission passed:

> AGENT

Only `session-start` and `turn-start` fire — no `turn-end` or `session-end`. And `checkpoint_count` is 0. The hooks still aren't completing. The session stays in `active` phase — no `turn-end` means the checkpoint never gets created.

Two issues:
1. **File written to wrong path** — the `../../` relative path with "Claude Code" in it means the file doesn't end up in the test repo
2. **No turn-end hook fires** — OpenCode doesn't emit `session.idle` event, so the plugin never calls the `turn-end` hook

Let me check if there's a `session.idle` or `session.deleted` event in the raw opencode output:

> AGENT

Those are just from the plugin file dump. No actual `session.idle` event fires. So there are two issues:

1. **`session.idle` event not emitted in `run` mode** — the plugin never gets the signal to call `turn-end`
2. **File path corruption** — `Claude Code` gets injected into the path

These are both OpenCode bugs. Let me summarize what we've found for you:

---

**Summary of findings:**

The `external_directory` permission issue is fixable with `"external_directory": "allow"` in `opencode.json`. But there are two remaining OpenCode bugs that prevent E2E tests from working:

1. **No `session.idle` event in `run` mode** — OpenCode's `run` command doesn't emit the `session.idle` event that triggers the plugin to call `turn-end`. Without `turn-end`, no checkpoint is created. Only `session-start` and `turn-start` fire.

2. **Path corruption** — OpenCode injects the model agent type ("Claude Code") into file paths, causing writes to go to wrong directories (e.g., `../../TestE2E_DebugClaude CodeHooks.../hello.go` instead of `./hello.go`).

Issue #1 is the blocker — even if files were created correctly, we'd have no checkpoints. We could work around this by manually calling the `turn-end` and `session-end` hooks after `opencode run` completes in the `OpenCodeRunner`, but that would only partially simulate the real workflow. Want me to try that approach, or should we file these as OpenCode issues and skip OpenCode E2E tests for now?

diff --git a/cmd/entire/cli/agent/opencode/entire_plugin.ts b/cmd/entire/cli/agent/opencode/entire_plugin.ts
index c44abc54d..f4cd267aa 100644
--- a/cmd/entire/cli/agent/opencode/entire_plugin.ts
+++ b/cmd/entire/cli/agent/opencode/entire_plugin.ts

 // Requires Bun runtime (used by OpenCode's plugin system for loading ESM plugins).
 import type { Plugin } from "@opencode-ai/plugin"
 
-export const EntirePlugin: Plugin = async ({ $, directory }) => {
+// Spawns a child process connected to the parent via stdin pipe.
+// When the parent exits (including SIGKILL), the pipe closes and the child
+// calls `entire hooks opencode session-end` for every tracked session.
+function spawnCleanupWatcher(entireCmd: string) {
+  const script = `
+    const { execFileSync } = require("child_process");
+    const cmd = ${JSON.stringify(entireCmd)};
+    const sessions = new Set();
+    let buf = "";
+    process.stdin.setEncoding("utf8");
+    process.stdin.on("data", (chunk) => {
+      buf += chunk;
+      let i;
+      while ((i = buf.indexOf("\\n")) !== -1) {
+        try {
+          const msg = JSON.parse(buf.slice(0, i));
+          if (msg.type === "track") sessions.add(msg.id);
+          else if (msg.type === "untrack") sessions.delete(msg.id);
+        } catch {}
+        buf = buf.slice(i + 1);
+      }
+    });
+    process.stdin.on("end", () => {
+      for (const id of sessions) {
+        try {
+          execFileSync(cmd, ["hooks", "opencode", "session-end"], {
+            input: JSON.stringify({ session_id: id }),
+            timeout: 1000,
+          });
+        } catch {}
+      }
+    });
+  `;
+
+  try {
+    const child = Bun.spawn(["node", "-e", script], {
+      stdin: "pipe",
+      stdout: "ignore",
+      stderr: "ignore",
+    });
+    const send = (type: string, id: string) => {
+      try { child.stdin.write(JSON.stringify({ type, id }) + "\n"); child.stdin.flush(); } catch { }
+    };
+    return {
+      track: (id: string) => send("track", id),
+      untrack: (id: string) => send("untrack", id),
+    };
+  } catch {
+    return { track: () => { }, untrack: () => { } };
+  }
+}
+
+export const EntirePlugin: Plugin = async ({ $ }) => {
   const ENTIRE_CMD = "__ENTIRE_CMD__"
   // Track seen user messages to fire turn-start only once per message
   const seenUserMessages = new Set<string>()

> AGENT

This PR adds a cleanup watcher that spawns a child process connected to the parent via stdin pipe. When `opencode run` exits (even via SIGKILL), the pipe closes and the child calls `entire hooks opencode session-end` for every tracked session.

**Would it help with issue #1?** Partially — it ensures `session-end` fires when `opencode run` exits. But the missing hook is **`turn-end`** (triggered by `session.idle`), not `session-end`. The checkpoint/SaveStep happens during `turn-end`. The lifecycle is:

```
session-start → turn-start → [agent works] → turn-end (saves checkpoint) → session-end
```

Right now we get `session-start` and `turn-start` but no `turn-end`, so no checkpoint is created. This PR would add the missing `session-end` but the `turn-end` gap would remain.

The key question is: does the `session-end` handler also trigger a checkpoint save when the session is still `active` (turn-end never fired)? Let me check.

    18→	HookNameSessionEnd   = "session-end"

    24→// HookNames returns the hook verbs this agent supports.
    25→func (a *OpenCodeAgent) HookNames() []string {
    26→	return []string{
    27→		HookNameSessionStart,
    28→		HookNameSessionEnd,
    29→		HookNameTurnStart,
    30→		HookNameTurnEnd,
    31→		HookNameCompaction,
    32→	}
    33→}
    34→
    35→// ParseHookEvent translates OpenCode hook calls into normalized lifecycle events.
    36→func (a *OpenCodeAgent) ParseHookEvent(hookName string, stdin io.Reader) (*agent.Event, error) {
    37→	switch hookName {
    38→	case HookNameSessionStart:
    39→		raw, err := agent.ReadAndParseHookInput[sessionInfoRaw](stdin)
    40→		if err != nil {
    41→			return nil, err
    42→		}
    43→		return &agent.Event{
    44→			Type:      agent.SessionStart,
    45→			SessionID: raw.SessionID,
    46→			Timestamp: time.Now(),
    47→		}, nil
    48→
    49→	case HookNameTurnStart:
    50→		raw, err := agent.ReadAndParseHookInput[turnStartRaw](stdin)
    51→		if err != nil {
    52→			return nil, err
    53→		}
    54→		// Get the temp file path for this session (may not exist yet, but needed for pre-prompt state).
    55→		repoRoot, err := paths.RepoRoot()
    56→		if err != nil {
    57→			repoRoot = "."
    58→		}
    59→		tmpDir := filepath.Join(repoRoot, paths.EntireTmpDir)
    60→		transcriptPath := filepath.Join(tmpDir, raw.SessionID+".json")
    61→		return &agent.Event{
    62→			Type:       agent.TurnStart,
    63→			SessionID:  raw.SessionID,
    64→			SessionRef: transcriptPath,
    65→			Prompt:     raw.Prompt,
    66→			Timestamp:  time.Now(),
    67→		}, nil
    68→
    69→	case HookNameTurnEnd:
    70→		raw, err := agent.ReadAndParseHookInput[sessionInfoRaw](stdin)
    71→		if err != nil {
    72→			return nil, err
    73→		}
    74→		// Call `opencode export` to get the transcript and write to temp file
    75→		transcriptPath, exportErr := a.fetchAndCacheExport(raw.SessionID)
    76→		if exportErr != nil {
    77→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to export session: %w", exportErr)
    78→		}
    79→		return &agent.Event{
    80→			Type:       agent.TurnEnd,
    81→			SessionID:  raw.SessionID,
    82→			SessionRef: transcriptPath,
    83→			Timestamp:  time.Now(),
    84→		}, nil
    85→
    86→	case HookNameCompaction:
    87→		raw, err := agent.ReadAndParseHookInput[sessionInfoRaw](stdin)
    88→		if err != nil {
    89→			return nil, err
    90→		}
    91→		return &agent.Event{
    92→			Type:      agent.Compaction,
    93→			SessionID: raw.SessionID,
    94→			Timestamp: time.Now(),
    95→		}, nil
    96→
    97→	case HookNameSessionEnd:
    98→		raw, err := agent.ReadAndParseHookInput[sessionInfoRaw](stdin)
    99→		if err != nil {
   100→			return nil, err
   101→		}
   102→		return &agent.Event{
   103→			Type:      agent.SessionEnd,
   104→			SessionID: raw.SessionID,
   105→			Timestamp: time.Now(),
   106→		}, nil
   107→
   108→	default:
   109→		return nil, nil //nolint:nilnil // nil event = no lifecycle action for unknown hooks
   110→	}
   111→}
   112→
   113→// fetchAndCacheExport calls `opencode export <sessionID>` and writes the result
   114→// to a temporary file. Returns the path to the temp file.
   115→//
   116→// Integration testing: Set ENTIRE_TEST_OPENCODE_MOCK_EXPORT=1 to skip the
   117→// `opencode export` call and use pre-written mock data instead. Tests must
   118→// pre-write the transcript file to .entire/tmp/<sessionID>.json before
   119→// triggering the hook. See integration_test/hooks.go:SimulateOpenCodeTurnEnd.
   120→func (a *OpenCodeAgent) fetchAndCacheExport(sessionID string) (string, error) {
   121→	// Get repo root for the temp directory
   122→	repoRoot, err := paths.RepoRoot()
   123→	if err != nil {
   124→		repoRoot = "."
   125→	}
   126→
   127→	tmpDir := filepath.Join(repoRoot, paths.EntireTmpDir)
   128→	tmpFile := filepath.Join(tmpDir, sessionID+".json")
   129→
   130→	// Integration test mode: use pre-written mock file without calling opencode export
   131→	if os.Getenv("ENTIRE_TEST_OPENCODE_MOCK_EXPORT") != "" {
   132→		if _, err := os.Stat(tmpFile); err == nil {
   133→			return tmpFile, nil
   134→		}
   135→		return "", fmt.Errorf("mock export file not found: %s (ENTIRE_TEST_OPENCODE_MOCK_EXPORT is set)", tmpFile)
   136→	}
   137→
   138→	// Call opencode export to get the transcript (always refresh on each turn)
   139→	data, err := runOpenCodeExport(sessionID)
   140→	if err != nil {
   141→		return "", fmt.Errorf("opencode export failed: %w", err)
   142→	}
   143→
   144→	// Validate output is valid JSON before caching
   145→	if !json.Valid(data) {
   146→		return "", fmt.Errorf("opencode export returned invalid JSON (%d bytes)", len(data))
   147→	}
   148→
   149→	// Write to temp directory under .entire
   150→	if err := os.MkdirAll(tmpDir, 0o750); err != nil {
   151→		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to create temp dir: %w", err)
   152→	}
   153→
   154→	if err := os.WriteFile(tmpFile, data, 0o600); err != nil {
   155→		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to write export file: %w", err)
   156→	}
   157→
   158→	return tmpFile, nil
   159→}
   160→

     1→//go:generate go run gen_state_diagram.go

     3→package session
     4→
     5→import (
     6→	"context"
     7→	"fmt"

    10→	"time"

    13→)
    14→
    15→// Phase represents the lifecycle stage of a session.
    16→type Phase string
    17→
    18→const (
    19→	PhaseActive Phase = "active"
    20→	PhaseIdle   Phase = "idle"
    21→	PhaseEnded  Phase = "ended"
    22→)
    23→
    24→// allPhases is the canonical list of phases for enumeration (e.g., diagram generation).
    25→var allPhases = []Phase{PhaseIdle, PhaseActive, PhaseEnded}
    26→
    27→// PhaseFromString normalizes a phase string, treating empty or unknown values
    28→// as PhaseIdle for backward compatibility with pre-state-machine session files.
    29→func PhaseFromString(s string) Phase {
    30→	switch Phase(s) {
    31→	case PhaseActive, "active_committed":
    32→		// "active_committed" was removed with the 1:1 checkpoint model.
    33→		// It previously meant "agent active + commit happened during turn".
    34→		// Normalize to ACTIVE so HandleTurnEnd can finalize any pending checkpoints.
    35→		return PhaseActive
    36→	case PhaseIdle:
    37→		return PhaseIdle
    38→	case PhaseEnded:
    39→		return PhaseEnded
    40→	default:
    41→		// Backward compat: truly unknown phases normalize to idle.
    42→		return PhaseIdle
    43→	}
    44→}
    45→
    46→// IsActive reports whether the phase represents an active agent turn.
    47→func (p Phase) IsActive() bool {
    48→	return p == PhaseActive
    49→}
    50→
    51→// Event represents something that happened to a session.
    52→type Event int
    53→
    54→const (
    55→	EventTurnStart    Event = iota // Agent begins working on a prompt
    56→	EventTurnEnd                   // Agent finishes its turn
    57→	EventGitCommit                 // A git commit was made (PostCommit hook)
    58→	EventSessionStart              // Session process started (SessionStart hook)
    59→	EventSessionStop               // Session process ended (SessionStop hook)
    60→	EventCompaction                // Agent compacted context mid-turn (PreCompress hook)
    61→)
    62→
    63→// allEvents is the canonical list of events for enumeration.
    64→var allEvents = []Event{EventTurnStart, EventTurnEnd, EventGitCommit, EventSessionStart, EventSessionStop, EventCompaction}
    65→
    66→// String returns a human-readable name for the event.
    67→func (e Event) String() string {
    68→	switch e {
    69→	case EventTurnStart:
    70→		return "TurnStart"
    71→	case EventTurnEnd:
    72→		return "TurnEnd"
    73→	case EventGitCommit:
    74→		return "GitCommit"
    75→	case EventSessionStart:
    76→		return "SessionStart"
    77→	case EventSessionStop:
    78→		return "SessionStop"
    79→	case EventCompaction:
    80→		return "Compaction"
    81→	default:
    82→		return fmt.Sprintf("Event(%d)", int(e))
    83→	}
    84→}
    85→
    86→// Action represents a side effect that should be performed after a transition.
    87→// The caller is responsible for executing these -- the state machine only declares them.
    88→type Action int
    89→
    90→const (
    91→	ActionCondense               Action = iota // Condense session data to permanent storage
    92→	ActionCondenseIfFilesTouched               // Condense only if FilesTouched is non-empty
    93→	ActionDiscardIfNoFiles                     // Discard session if FilesTouched is empty
    94→	ActionWarnStaleSession                     // Warn user about stale session(s)
    95→	ActionClearEndedAt                         // Clear EndedAt timestamp (session re-entering)
    96→	ActionUpdateLastInteraction                // Update LastInteractionTime
    97→)
    98→
    99→// String returns a human-readable name for the action.
   100→func (a Action) String() string {
   101→	switch a {
   102→	case ActionCondense:
   103→		return "Condense"
   104→	case ActionCondenseIfFilesTouched:
   105→		return "CondenseIfFilesTouched"
   106→	case ActionDiscardIfNoFiles:
   107→		return "DiscardIfNoFiles"
   108→	case ActionWarnStaleSession:
   109→		return "WarnStaleSession"
   110→	case ActionClearEndedAt:
   111→		return "ClearEndedAt"
   112→	case ActionUpdateLastInteraction:
   113→		return "UpdateLastInteraction"
   114→	default:
   115→		return fmt.Sprintf("Action(%d)", int(a))
   116→	}
   117→}
   118→
   119→// TransitionContext provides read-only context for transitions that need
   120→// to inspect session state without mutating it.
   121→type TransitionContext struct {
   122→	HasFilesTouched    bool // len(FilesTouched) > 0
   123→	IsRebaseInProgress bool // .git/rebase-merge/ or .git/rebase-apply/ exists
   124→}
   125→
   126→// TransitionResult holds the outcome of a state machine transition.
   127→type TransitionResult struct {
   128→	NewPhase Phase
   129→	Actions  []Action
   130→}
   131→
   132→// Transition computes the next phase and required actions given the current
   133→// phase and an event. This is a pure function with no side effects.
   134→//
   135→// Empty or unknown phase values are normalized to PhaseIdle for backward
   136→// compatibility with session state files created before phase tracking.
   137→func Transition(current Phase, event Event, ctx TransitionContext) TransitionResult {
   138→	// Normalize empty/unknown phase to idle for backward compatibility.
   139→	current = PhaseFromString(string(current))
   140→
   141→	switch current {
   142→	case PhaseIdle:
   143→		return transitionFromIdle(event, ctx)
   144→	case PhaseActive:
   145→		return transitionFromActive(event, ctx)
   146→	case PhaseEnded:
   147→		return transitionFromEnded(event, ctx)
   148→	default:
   149→		// PhaseFromString guarantees we only get known phases, but the
   150→		// exhaustive linter requires handling the default case.
   151→		return TransitionResult{NewPhase: PhaseIdle}
   152→	}
   153→}
   154→
   155→func transitionFromIdle(event Event, ctx TransitionContext) TransitionResult {
   156→	switch event {
   157→	case EventTurnStart:
   158→		return TransitionResult{
   159→			NewPhase: PhaseActive,
   160→			Actions:  []Action{ActionUpdateLastInteraction},
   161→		}
   162→	case EventTurnEnd:
   163→		// Turn end while idle is a no-op (no active turn to end).
   164→		return TransitionResult{NewPhase: PhaseIdle}
   165→	case EventGitCommit:
   166→		if ctx.IsRebaseInProgress {
   167→			return TransitionResult{NewPhase: PhaseIdle}
   168→		}
   169→		return TransitionResult{
   170→			NewPhase: PhaseIdle,
   171→			Actions:  []Action{ActionCondense, ActionUpdateLastInteraction},
   172→		}
   173→	case EventSessionStart:
   174→		// Already condensable, no-op.
   175→		return TransitionResult{NewPhase: PhaseIdle}
   176→	case EventSessionStop:
   177→		return TransitionResult{
   178→			NewPhase: PhaseEnded,
   179→			Actions:  []Action{ActionUpdateLastInteraction},
   180→		}
   181→	case EventCompaction:
   182→		// Compaction while idle shouldn't happen, but condense if there's work.
   183→		return TransitionResult{
   184→			NewPhase: PhaseIdle,
   185→			Actions:  []Action{ActionCondenseIfFilesTouched, ActionUpdateLastInteraction},
   186→		}
   187→	default:
   188→		return TransitionResult{NewPhase: PhaseIdle}
   189→	}
   190→}
   191→
   192→func transitionFromActive(event Event, ctx TransitionContext) TransitionResult {
   193→	switch event {
   194→	case EventTurnStart:
   195→		// Ctrl-C recovery: agent crashed or user interrupted mid-turn.
   196→		return TransitionResult{
   197→			NewPhase: PhaseActive,
   198→			Actions:  []Action{ActionUpdateLastInteraction},
   199→		}
   200→	case EventTurnEnd:
   201→		return TransitionResult{
   202→			NewPhase: PhaseIdle,
   203→			Actions:  []Action{ActionUpdateLastInteraction},
   204→		}
   205→	case EventGitCommit:
   206→		if ctx.IsRebaseInProgress {
   207→			return TransitionResult{NewPhase: PhaseActive}
   208→		}
   209→		return TransitionResult{
   210→			NewPhase: PhaseActive,
   211→			Actions:  []Action{ActionCondense, ActionUpdateLastInteraction},
   212→		}
   213→	case EventSessionStart:
   214→		return TransitionResult{
   215→			NewPhase: PhaseActive,
   216→			Actions:  []Action{ActionWarnStaleSession},
   217→		}
   218→	case EventSessionStop:
   219→		return TransitionResult{
   220→			NewPhase: PhaseEnded,
   221→			Actions:  []Action{ActionUpdateLastInteraction},
   222→		}
   223→	case EventCompaction:
   224→		// Compaction mid-turn: save current progress but stay active.
   225→		// The transcript offset will be reset by the compaction handler.
   226→		return TransitionResult{
   227→			NewPhase: PhaseActive,
   228→			Actions:  []Action{ActionCondenseIfFilesTouched, ActionUpdateLastInteraction},
   229→		}
   230→	default:
   231→		return TransitionResult{NewPhase: PhaseActive}
   232→	}
   233→}
   234→
   235→func transitionFromEnded(event Event, ctx TransitionContext) TransitionResult {
   236→	switch event {
   237→	case EventTurnStart:
   238→		return TransitionResult{
   239→			NewPhase: PhaseActive,
   240→			Actions:  []Action{ActionClearEndedAt, ActionUpdateLastInteraction},
   241→		}
   242→	case EventTurnEnd:
   243→		// Turn end while ended is a no-op.
   244→		return TransitionResult{NewPhase: PhaseEnded}
   245→	case EventGitCommit:
   246→		if ctx.IsRebaseInProgress {
   247→			return TransitionResult{NewPhase: PhaseEnded}
   248→		}
   249→		if ctx.HasFilesTouched {
   250→			return TransitionResult{
   251→				NewPhase: PhaseEnded,
   252→				Actions:  []Action{ActionCondenseIfFilesTouched, ActionUpdateLastInteraction},
   253→			}
   254→		}
   255→		return TransitionResult{
   256→			NewPhase: PhaseEnded,
   257→			Actions:  []Action{ActionDiscardIfNoFiles, ActionUpdateLastInteraction},
   258→		}
   259→	case EventSessionStart:
   260→		return TransitionResult{
   261→			NewPhase: PhaseIdle,
   262→			Actions:  []Action{ActionClearEndedAt},
   263→		}
   264→	case EventSessionStop:
   265→		// Already ended, no-op.
   266→		return TransitionResult{NewPhase: PhaseEnded}
   267→	case EventCompaction:
   268→		// Compaction while ended shouldn't happen, no-op.
   269→		return TransitionResult{NewPhase: PhaseEnded}
   270→	default:
   271→		return TransitionResult{NewPhase: PhaseEnded}
   272→	}
   273→}
   274→
   275→// ActionHandler defines strategy-specific side effects for state transitions.
   276→// The compiler enforces that every strategy-specific action has a handler.
   277→type ActionHandler interface {
   278→	HandleCondense(state *State) error
   279→	HandleCondenseIfFilesTouched(state *State) error
   280→	HandleDiscardIfNoFiles(state *State) error
   281→	HandleWarnStaleSession(state *State) error
   282→}
   283→
   284→// NoOpActionHandler is a default ActionHandler where all methods are no-ops.
   285→// Embed this in handler structs to only override the methods you need.
   286→type NoOpActionHandler struct{}
   287→
   288→func (NoOpActionHandler) HandleCondense(_ *State) error               { return nil }
   289→func (NoOpActionHandler) HandleCondenseIfFilesTouched(_ *State) error { return nil }
   290→func (NoOpActionHandler) HandleDiscardIfNoFiles(_ *State) error       { return nil }
   291→func (NoOpActionHandler) HandleWarnStaleSession(_ *State) error       { return nil }
   292→
   293→// ApplyTransition applies a TransitionResult to state: sets the new phase
   294→// unconditionally, then executes all actions. Common actions
   295→// (UpdateLastInteraction, ClearEndedAt) always run regardless of handler
   296→// errors so that bookkeeping fields stay consistent with the new phase.
   297→// Strategy-specific handler actions stop on the first error; subsequent
   298→// handler actions are skipped but common actions continue. Returns the
   299→// first handler error, or nil.
   300→func ApplyTransition(state *State, result TransitionResult, handler ActionHandler) error {
   301→	logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "session")
   302→
   303→	actionStrs := make([]string, len(result.Actions))
   304→	for i, a := range result.Actions {
   305→		actionStrs[i] = a.String()
   306→	}
   307→	logging.Debug(logCtx, "ApplyTransition",
   308→		slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
   309→		slog.String("old_phase", string(state.Phase)),
   310→		slog.String("new_phase", string(result.NewPhase)),
   311→		slog.String("actions", strings.Join(actionStrs, ",")),
   312→	)
   313→
   314→	state.Phase = result.NewPhase
   315→
   316→	var handlerErr error
   317→	for _, action := range result.Actions {
   318→		switch action {
   319→		// Common actions: always applied, even after a handler error.
   320→		case ActionUpdateLastInteraction:
   321→			now := time.Now()
   322→			state.LastInteractionTime = &now
   323→		case ActionClearEndedAt:
   324→			state.EndedAt = nil
   325→
   326→		// Strategy-specific actions: skip remaining after the first handler error.
   327→		case ActionCondense:
   328→			if handlerErr == nil {
   329→				if err := handler.HandleCondense(state); err != nil {
   330→					handlerErr = fmt.Errorf("%s: %w", action, err)
   331→				}
   332→			}
   333→		case ActionCondenseIfFilesTouched:
   334→			if handlerErr == nil {
   335→				if err := handler.HandleCondenseIfFilesTouched(state); err != nil {
   336→					handlerErr = fmt.Errorf("%s: %w", action, err)
   337→				}
   338→			}
   339→		case ActionDiscardIfNoFiles:
   340→			if handlerErr == nil {
   341→				if err := handler.HandleDiscardIfNoFiles(state); err != nil {
   342→					handlerErr = fmt.Errorf("%s: %w", action, err)
   343→				}
   344→			}
   345→		case ActionWarnStaleSession:
   346→			if handlerErr == nil {
   347→				if err := handler.HandleWarnStaleSession(state); err != nil {
   348→					handlerErr = fmt.Errorf("%s: %w", action, err)
   349→				}
   350→			}
   351→		default:
   352→			if handlerErr == nil {
   353→				handlerErr = fmt.Errorf("unhandled action: %s", action)
   354→			}
   355→		}
   356→	}
   357→	return handlerErr
   358→}
   359→
   360→// MermaidDiagram generates a Mermaid state diagram from the transition table.
   361→// The diagram is derived by calling Transition() for representative context
   362→// variants per phase/event, so it stays in sync with the implementation.
   363→func MermaidDiagram() string {
   364→	var b strings.Builder
   365→	b.WriteString("stateDiagram-v2\n")
   366→
   367→	// State declarations with descriptions.
   368→	b.WriteString("    state \"IDLE\" as idle\n")
   369→	b.WriteString("    state \"ACTIVE\" as active\n")
   370→	b.WriteString("    state \"ENDED\" as ended\n")
   371→	b.WriteString("\n")
   372→
   373→	// Context variants for GitCommit: rebase, files/no-files.
   374→	type contextVariant struct {
   375→		label string
   376→		ctx   TransitionContext
   377→	}
   378→
   379→	// For each phase/event, generate edges.
   380→	for _, phase := range allPhases {
   381→		for _, event := range allEvents {
   382→			var variants []contextVariant
   383→			switch {
   384→			case event == EventGitCommit && phase == PhaseEnded:
   385→				// HasFilesTouched only affects PhaseEnded transitions;
   386→				// other phases produce identical edges for both values.
   387→				variants = []contextVariant{
   388→					{"[files]", TransitionContext{HasFilesTouched: true}},
   389→					{"[no files]", TransitionContext{HasFilesTouched: false}},
   390→					{"[rebase]", TransitionContext{IsRebaseInProgress: true}},
   391→				}
   392→			case event == EventGitCommit:
   393→				variants = []contextVariant{
   394→					{"", TransitionContext{}},
   395→					{"[rebase]", TransitionContext{IsRebaseInProgress: true}},
   396→				}
   397→			default:
   398→				variants = []contextVariant{
   399→					{"", TransitionContext{}},
   400→				}
   401→			}
   402→
   403→			for _, v := range variants {
   404→				result := Transition(phase, event, v.ctx)
   405→
   406→				// Build edge label.
   407→				label := event.String()
   408→				if v.label != "" {
   409→					label += " " + v.label
   410→				}
   411→				if len(result.Actions) > 0 {
   412→					actionNames := make([]string, 0, len(result.Actions))
   413→					for _, a := range result.Actions {
   414→						actionNames = append(actionNames, a.String())
   415→					}
   416→					label += " / " + strings.Join(actionNames, ", ")
   417→				}
   418→
   419→				from := string(phase)
   420→				to := string(result.NewPhase)
   421→
   422→				fmt.Fprintf(&b, "    %s --> %s : %s\n", from, to, label)
   423→			}
   424→		}
   425→	}
   426→
   427→	return b.String()
   428→}
   429→
```

> AGENT

Now I can answer definitively. Looking at the state machine:

**ACTIVE + SessionStop → ENDED** with only `ActionUpdateLastInteraction`:

```go
case EventSessionStop:
    return TransitionResult{
        NewPhase: PhaseEnded,
        Actions:  []Action{ActionUpdateLastInteraction},
    }
```

**No `ActionCondense` or checkpoint save happens.** The session simply transitions to `ENDED` without saving anything. The checkpoint/SaveStep only happens during:
- `TurnEnd` (via the hook handler, not the state machine)
- `GitCommit` → `ActionCondense`
- `Compaction` → `ActionCondenseIfFilesTouched`

So **PR #462 would NOT help with issue #1**. It ensures `session-end` fires on process exit, but `session-end` (SessionStop) only marks the session as ended — it doesn't trigger a checkpoint save. The missing `turn-end` is still the blocker.

The flow we need is:
```
turn-end → fetchAndCacheExport() → SaveStep() → checkpoint created
```

And `session.idle` (which triggers `turn-end`) simply doesn't fire in `opencode run` mode.

# OpenCode Plugin Event Types

Based on the documentation, here are all available plugin event types organized by category:

**Command Events**
- `command.executed`

**File Events**
- `file.edited`
- `file.watcher.updated`

**Installation Events**
- `installation.updated`

**LSP Events**
- `lsp.client.diagnostics`
- `lsp.updated`

**Message Events**
- `message.part.removed`
- `message.part.updated`
- `message.removed`
- `message.updated`

**Permission Events**
- `permission.asked`
- `permission.replied`

**Server Events**
- `server.connected`

**Session Events**
- `session.created`
- `session.compacted`
- `session.deleted`
- `session.diff`
- `session.error`
- `session.idle`
- `session.status`
- `session.updated`

**Todo Events**
- `todo.updated`

**Shell Events**
- `shell.env`

**Tool Events**
- `tool.execute.after`
- `tool.execute.before`

**TUI Events**
- `tui.prompt.append`
- `tui.command.execute`
- `tui.toast.show`

**Experimental Events**
- `experimental.session.compacting`

Note: The documentation doesn't include specific events for "message completion" or "assistant response finished" as distinct event types—these concepts are covered by the `message.updated`, `session.idle`, and `session.status` events.
```

import type { Argv } from "yargs"
import path from "path"
import { pathToFileURL } from "bun"
import { UI } from "../ui"
import { cmd } from "./cmd"
import { Flag } from "../../flag/flag"
import { bootstrap } from "../bootstrap"
import { EOL } from "os"
import { Filesystem } from "../../util/filesystem"
import { createOpencodeClient, type Message, type OpencodeClient, type ToolPart } from "@opencode-ai/sdk/v2"
import { Server } from "../../server/server"
import { Provider } from "../../provider/provider"
import { Agent } from "../../agent/agent"
import { PermissionNext } from "../../permission/next"
import { Tool } from "../../tool/tool"
import { GlobTool } from "../../tool/glob"
import { GrepTool } from "../../tool/grep"
import { ListTool } from "../../tool/ls"
import { ReadTool } from "../../tool/read"
import { WebFetchTool } from "../../tool/webfetch"
import { EditTool } from "../../tool/edit"
import { WriteTool } from "../../tool/write"
import { CodeSearchTool } from "../../tool/codesearch"
import { WebSearchTool } from "../../tool/websearch"
import { TaskTool } from "../../tool/task"
import { SkillTool } from "../../tool/skill"
import { BashTool } from "../../tool/bash"
import { TodoWriteTool } from "../../tool/todo"
import { Locale } from "../../util/locale"

type ToolProps<T extends Tool.Info> = {
  input: Tool.InferParameters<T>
  metadata: Tool.InferMetadata<T>
  part: ToolPart
}

function props<T extends Tool.Info>(part: ToolPart): ToolProps<T> {
  const state = part.state
  return {
    input: state.input as Tool.InferParameters<T>,
    metadata: ("metadata" in state ? state.metadata : {}) as Tool.InferMetadata<T>,
    part,
  }
}

type Inline = {
  icon: string
  title: string
  description?: string
}

function inline(info: Inline) {
  const suffix = info.description ? UI.Style.TEXT_DIM + ` ${info.description}` + UI.Style.TEXT_NORMAL : ""
  UI.println(UI.Style.TEXT_NORMAL + info.icon, UI.Style.TEXT_NORMAL + info.title + suffix)
}

function block(info: Inline, output?: string) {
  UI.empty()
  inline(info)
  if (!output?.trim()) return
  UI.println(output)
  UI.empty()
}

function fallback(part: ToolPart) {
  const state = part.state
  const input = "input" in state ? state.input : undefined
  const title =
    ("title" in state && state.title ? state.title : undefined) ||
    (input && typeof input === "object" && Object.keys(input).length > 0 ? JSON.stringify(input) : "Unknown")
  inline({
    icon: "⚙",
    title: `${part.tool} ${title}`,
  })
}

function glob(info: ToolProps<typeof GlobTool>) {
  const root = info.input.path ?? ""
  const title = `Glob "${info.input.pattern}"`
  const suffix = root ? `in ${normalizePath(root)}` : ""
  const num = info.metadata.count
  const description =
    num === undefined ? suffix : `${suffix}${suffix ? " · " : ""}${num} ${num === 1 ? "match" : "matches"}`
  inline({
    icon: "✱",
    title,
    ...(description && { description }),
  })
}

function grep(info: ToolProps<typeof GrepTool>) {
  const root = info.input.path ?? ""
  const title = `Grep "${info.input.pattern}"`
  const suffix = root ? `in ${normalizePath(root)}` : ""
  const num = info.metadata.matches
  const description =
    num === undefined ? suffix : `${suffix}${suffix ? " · " : ""}${num} ${num === 1 ? "match" : "matches"}`
  inline({
    icon: "✱",
    title,
    ...(description && { description }),
  })
}

function list(info: ToolProps<typeof ListTool>) {
  const dir = info.input.path ? normalizePath(info.input.path) : ""
  inline({
    icon: "→",
    title: dir ? `List ${dir}` : "List",
  })
}

function read(info: ToolProps<typeof ReadTool>) {
  const file = normalizePath(info.input.filePath)
  const pairs = Object.entries(info.input).filter(([key, value]) => {
    if (key === "filePath") return false
    return typeof value === "string" || typeof value === "number" || typeof value === "boolean"
  })
  const description = pairs.length ? `[${pairs.map(([key, value]) => `${key}=${value}`).join(", ")}]` : undefined
  inline({
    icon: "→",
    title: `Read ${file}`,
    ...(description && { description }),
  })
}

function write(info: ToolProps<typeof WriteTool>) {
  block(
    {
      icon: "←",
      title: `Write ${normalizePath(info.input.filePath)}`,
    },
    info.part.state.status === "completed" ? info.part.state.output : undefined,
  )
}

function webfetch(info: ToolProps<typeof WebFetchTool>) {
  inline({
    icon: "%",
    title: `WebFetch ${info.input.url}`,
  })
}

function edit(info: ToolProps<typeof EditTool>) {
  const title = normalizePath(info.input.filePath)
  const diff = info.metadata.diff
  block(
    {
      icon: "←",
      title: `Edit ${title}`,
    },
    diff,
  )
}

function codesearch(info: ToolProps<typeof CodeSearchTool>) {
  inline({
    icon: "◇",
    title: `Exa Code Search "${info.input.query}"`,
  })
}

function websearch(info: ToolProps<typeof WebSearchTool>) {
  inline({
    icon: "◈",
    title: `Exa Web Search "${info.input.query}"`,
  })
}

function task(info: ToolProps<typeof TaskTool>) {
  const input = info.part.state.input
  const status = info.part.state.status
  const subagent =
    typeof input.subagent_type === "string" && input.subagent_type.trim().length > 0 ? input.subagent_type : "unknown"
  const agent = Locale.titlecase(subagent)
  const desc =
    typeof input.description === "string" && input.description.trim().length > 0 ? input.description : undefined
  const icon = status === "error" ? "✗" : status === "running" ? "•" : "✓"
  const name = desc ?? `${agent} Task`
  inline({
    icon,
    title: name,
    description: desc ? `${agent} Agent` : undefined,
  })
}

function skill(info: ToolProps<typeof SkillTool>) {
  inline({
    icon: "→",
    title: `Skill "${info.input.name}"`,
  })
}

function bash(info: ToolProps<typeof BashTool>) {
  const output = info.part.state.status === "completed" ? info.part.state.output?.trim() : undefined
  block(
    {
      icon: "$",
      title: `${info.input.command}`,
    },

            if (permission.sessionID !== sessionID) continue
            UI.println(
```

> AGENT

There it is. The `run` command itself uses `session.status` with `status.type === "idle"` to detect when the agent is done. This means `session.status` events **do** fire in run mode — the run command depends on them to know when to exit.

So we can listen for `session.status` where `status.type === "idle"` as an alternative to `session.idle`. Let me check if plugins receive this event too:

```
import { BusEvent } from "@/bus/bus-event"
import { Bus } from "@/bus"
import { Instance } from "@/project/instance"
import z from "zod"

export namespace SessionStatus {
  export const Info = z
    .union([
      z.object({
        type: z.literal("idle"),
      }),
      z.object({
        type: z.literal("retry"),
        attempt: z.number(),
        message: z.string(),
        next: z.number(),
      }),
      z.object({
        type: z.literal("busy"),
      }),
    ])
    .meta({
      ref: "SessionStatus",
    })
  export type Info = z.infer<typeof Info>

  export const Event = {
    Status: BusEvent.define(
      "session.status",
      z.object({
        sessionID: z.string(),
        status: Info,
      }),
    ),
    // deprecated
    Idle: BusEvent.define(
      "session.idle",
      z.object({
        sessionID: z.string(),
      }),
    ),
  }

  const state = Instance.state(() => {
    const data: Record<string, Info> = {}
    return data
  })

  export function get(sessionID: string) {
    return (
      state()[sessionID] ?? {
        type: "idle",
      }
    )
  }

  export function list() {
    return state()
  }

  export function set(sessionID: string, status: Info) {
    Bus.publish(Event.Status, {
      sessionID,
      status,
    })
    if (status.type === "idle") {
      // deprecated
      Bus.publish(Event.Idle, {
        sessionID,
      })
      delete state()[sessionID]
      return
    }
    state()[sessionID] = status
  }
}
```

> AGENT

This is the key finding:

```typescript
// deprecated
Idle: BusEvent.define(
  "session.idle",
  ...
),
```

And:
```typescript
if (status.type === "idle") {
  // deprecated
  Bus.publish(Event.Idle, { sessionID })
}
```

So `session.idle` is **deprecated** — it's still published when `session.status` with `type: "idle"` fires, but it's a secondary event. The `run` command uses `session.status` directly, not `session.idle`.

The issue is likely that in `run` mode, `session.idle` isn't reaching the plugin even though `session.status` is being published. Perhaps the plugin event bus doesn't relay `session.idle` in run mode, but does relay `session.status`.

**The fix**: update the plugin to listen for `session.status` (with `status.type === "idle"`) instead of (or in addition to) `session.idle` for the `turn-end` hook. This should work in `run` mode since the run command itself depends on this exact event.

Want me to update the `entire_plugin.ts` to use `session.status`?

     1→// Entire CLI plugin for OpenCode

     6→
     7→export const EntirePlugin: Plugin = async ({ $, directory }) => {
     8→  const ENTIRE_CMD = "__ENTIRE_CMD__"
     9→  // Track seen user messages to fire turn-start only once per message
    10→  const seenUserMessages = new Set<string>()
    11→  // Track current session ID for message events (which don't include sessionID)
    12→  let currentSessionID: string | null = null
    13→  // In-memory store for message metadata (role, tokens, etc.)
    14→  const messageStore = new Map<string, any>()
    15→
    16→  /**
    17→   * Pipe JSON payload to an entire hooks command.
    18→   * Errors are logged but never thrown — plugin failures must not crash OpenCode.
    19→   */
    20→  async function callHook(hookName: string, payload: Record<string, unknown>) {
    21→    try {
    22→      const json = JSON.stringify(payload)
    23→      await $`echo ${json} | ${ENTIRE_CMD} hooks opencode ${hookName}`.quiet().nothrow()
    24→    } catch {
    25→      // Silently ignore — plugin failures must not crash OpenCode
    26→    }
    27→  }
    28→
    29→  return {
    30→    event: async ({ event }) => {
    31→      switch (event.type) {
    32→        case "session.created": {
    33→          const session = (event as any).properties?.info
    34→          if (!session?.id) break
    35→          // Reset per-session tracking state when switching sessions.
    36→          if (currentSessionID !== session.id) {
    37→            seenUserMessages.clear()
    38→            messageStore.clear()
    39→          }
    40→          currentSessionID = session.id
    41→          await callHook("session-start", {
    42→            session_id: session.id,
    43→          })
    44→          break
    45→        }
    46→
    47→        case "message.updated": {
    48→          const msg = (event as any).properties?.info
    49→          if (!msg) break
    50→          // Store message metadata (role, time, tokens, etc.)
    51→          messageStore.set(msg.id, msg)
    52→          break
    53→        }
    54→
    55→        case "message.part.updated": {
    56→          const part = (event as any).properties?.part
    57→          if (!part?.messageID) break
    58→
    59→          // Fire turn-start on the first text part of a new user message
    60→          const msg = messageStore.get(part.messageID)
    61→          if (msg?.role === "user" && part.type === "text" && !seenUserMessages.has(msg.id)) {
    62→            seenUserMessages.add(msg.id)
    63→            const sessionID = msg.sessionID ?? currentSessionID
    64→            if (sessionID) {
    65→              await callHook("turn-start", {
    66→                session_id: sessionID,
    67→                prompt: part.text ?? "",
    68→              })
    69→            }
    70→          }
    71→          break
    72→        }
    73→
    74→        case "session.idle": {
    75→          const sessionID = (event as any).properties?.sessionID
    76→          if (!sessionID) break
    77→          // Go hook handler will call `opencode export` to get the transcript
    78→          await callHook("turn-end", {
    79→            session_id: sessionID,
    80→          })
    81→          break
    82→        }
    83→
    84→        case "session.compacted": {
    85→          const sessionID = (event as any).properties?.sessionID
    86→          if (!sessionID) break
    87→          await callHook("compaction", {
    88→            session_id: sessionID,
    89→          })
    90→          break
    91→        }
    92→
    93→        case "session.deleted": {
    94→          const session = (event as any).properties?.info
    95→          if (!session?.id) break
    96→          seenUserMessages.clear()
    97→          messageStore.clear()
    98→          currentSessionID = null
    99→          await callHook("session-end", {
   100→            session_id: session.id,
   101→          })
   102→          break
   103→        }
   104→      }
   105→    },
   106→  }
   107→}

    16→// AgentNameClaudeCode is the name for Claude Code agent.
    17→const AgentNameClaudeCode = "claude-code"

    19→// AgentNameGemini is the name for Gemini CLI agent.

    22→// AgentNameOpenCode is the name for OpenCode agent.

    24→
    25→// AgentRunner abstracts invoking a coding agent for e2e tests.
    26→// This follows the multi-agent pattern from cmd/entire/cli/agent/agent.go.
    27→type AgentRunner interface {
    28→	// Name returns the agent name (e.g., "claude-code", "gemini-cli")
    29→	Name() string
    30→
    31→	// IsAvailable checks if the agent CLI is installed and authenticated
    32→	IsAvailable() (bool, error)
    33→
    34→	// RunPrompt executes a prompt and returns the result
    35→	RunPrompt(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string) (*AgentResult, error)
    36→
    37→	// RunPromptWithTools executes with specific allowed tools
    38→	RunPromptWithTools(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string, tools []string) (*AgentResult, error)
    39→}
    40→
    41→// AgentResult holds the result of an agent invocation.
    42→type AgentResult struct {
    43→	Stdout   string
    44→	Stderr   string
    45→	ExitCode int
    46→	Duration time.Duration
    47→}
    48→
    49→// AgentRunnerConfig holds configuration for agent runners.
    50→type AgentRunnerConfig struct {
    51→	Model   string        // Model to use (e.g., "haiku" for Claude)
    52→	Timeout time.Duration // Timeout per prompt
    53→}
    54→
    55→// NewAgentRunner creates an agent runner based on the agent name.
    56→//
    57→//nolint:ireturn // factory pattern intentionally returns interface
    58→func NewAgentRunner(name string, config AgentRunnerConfig) AgentRunner {
    59→	switch name {
    60→	case AgentNameClaudeCode:
    61→		return NewClaudeCodeRunner(config)
    62→	case AgentNameGemini:
    63→		return NewGeminiCLIRunner(config)
    64→	case AgentNameOpenCode:
    65→		return NewOpenCodeRunner(config)
    66→	default:
    67→		// Return a runner that reports as unavailable
    68→		return &unavailableRunner{name: name}
    69→	}
    70→}
    71→
    72→// unavailableRunner is returned for unknown agent names.
    73→type unavailableRunner struct {
    74→	name string
    75→}
    76→
    77→func (r *unavailableRunner) Name() string { return r.name }
    78→
    79→func (r *unavailableRunner) IsAvailable() (bool, error) {
    80→	return false, fmt.Errorf("unknown agent: %s", r.name)
    81→}
    82→
    83→func (r *unavailableRunner) RunPrompt(_ context.Context, _ string, _ string) (*AgentResult, error) {
    84→	return nil, fmt.Errorf("agent %s is not available", r.name)
    85→}
    86→
    87→func (r *unavailableRunner) RunPromptWithTools(_ context.Context, _ string, _ string, _ []string) (*AgentResult, error) {
    88→	return nil, fmt.Errorf("agent %s is not available", r.name)
    89→}

    91→// ClaudeCodeRunner implements AgentRunner for Claude Code CLI.
    92→type ClaudeCodeRunner struct {
    93→	Model        string
    94→	Timeout      time.Duration
    95→	AllowedTools []string
    96→}
    97→
    98→// NewClaudeCodeRunner creates a new Claude Code runner with the given config.
    99→func NewClaudeCodeRunner(config AgentRunnerConfig) *ClaudeCodeRunner {
   100→	model := config.Model
   101→	if model == "" {
   102→		model = os.Getenv("E2E_CLAUDE_MODEL")
   103→		if model == "" {
   104→			model = "haiku"
   105→		}
   106→	}
   107→
   108→	timeout := config.Timeout
   109→	if timeout == 0 {
   110→		if envTimeout := os.Getenv("E2E_TIMEOUT"); envTimeout != "" {
   111→			if parsed, err := time.ParseDuration(envTimeout); err == nil {
   112→				timeout = parsed
   113→			}
   114→		}
   115→		if timeout == 0 {
   116→			timeout = 2 * time.Minute
   117→		}
   118→	}
   119→
   120→	return &ClaudeCodeRunner{
   121→		Model:        model,
   122→		Timeout:      timeout,
   123→		AllowedTools: []string{"Edit", "Read", "Write", "Bash", "Glob", "Grep"},
   124→	}
   125→}
   126→
   127→func (r *ClaudeCodeRunner) Name() string {
   128→	return AgentNameClaudeCode
   129→}
   130→
   131→// IsAvailable checks if Claude CLI is installed and responds to --version.
   132→// Note: This does NOT verify authentication status. Claude Code uses OAuth
   133→// authentication (via `claude login`), not ANTHROPIC_API_KEY. If the CLI is
   134→// installed but not logged in, tests will fail at RunPrompt time.
   135→func (r *ClaudeCodeRunner) IsAvailable() (bool, error) {
   136→	// Check if claude CLI is in PATH
   137→	if _, err := exec.LookPath("claude"); err != nil {
   138→		return false, fmt.Errorf("claude CLI not found in PATH: %w", err)
   139→	}
   140→
   141→	// Check if claude is working (--version doesn't require auth)
   142→	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 10*time.Second)
   143→	defer cancel()
   144→
   145→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "claude", "--version")
   146→	if err := cmd.Run(); err != nil {
   147→		return false, fmt.Errorf("claude CLI not working: %w", err)
   148→	}
   149→
   150→	return true, nil
   151→}
   152→
   153→func (r *ClaudeCodeRunner) RunPrompt(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string) (*AgentResult, error) {
   154→	return r.RunPromptWithTools(ctx, workDir, prompt, r.AllowedTools)
   155→}
   156→
   157→func (r *ClaudeCodeRunner) RunPromptWithTools(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string, tools []string) (*AgentResult, error) {
   158→	// Build command: claude --model <model> -p "<prompt>" --allowedTools <tools>
   159→	args := []string{
   160→		"--model", r.Model,
   161→		"-p", prompt,
   162→	}
   163→
   164→	if len(tools) > 0 {
   165→		// Claude CLI expects each tool as a separate argument after --allowedTools
   166→		// e.g., --allowedTools Edit Read Bash (not --allowedTools "Edit,Read,Bash")
   167→		args = append(args, "--allowedTools")
   168→		args = append(args, tools...)
   169→	}
   170→
   171→	// Create context with timeout
   172→	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, r.Timeout)
   173→	defer cancel()
   174→
   175→	//nolint:gosec // args are constructed from trusted config, not user input
   176→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "claude", args...)
   177→	cmd.Dir = workDir
   178→
   179→	var stdout, stderr bytes.Buffer
   180→	cmd.Stdout = &stdout
   181→	cmd.Stderr = &stderr
   182→
   183→	start := time.Now()
   184→	err := cmd.Run()
   185→	duration := time.Since(start)
   186→
   187→	result := &AgentResult{
   188→		Stdout:   stdout.String(),
   189→		Stderr:   stderr.String(),
   190→		Duration: duration,
   191→	}
   192→
   193→	if err != nil {
   194→		exitErr := &exec.ExitError{}
   195→		if errors.As(err, &exitErr) {
   196→			result.ExitCode = exitErr.ExitCode()
   197→		} else {
   198→			result.ExitCode = -1
   199→		}
   200→		//nolint:wrapcheck // error is from exec.Run, caller can check ExitCode in result
   201→		return result, err
   202→	}
   203→
   204→	result.ExitCode = 0
   205→	return result, nil
   206→}

   208→// GeminiCLIRunner implements AgentRunner for Gemini CLI.

   210→type GeminiCLIRunner struct {

   214→
   215→// NewGeminiCLIRunner creates a new Gemini CLI runner with the given config.
   216→func NewGeminiCLIRunner(config AgentRunnerConfig) *GeminiCLIRunner {
   217→	model := config.Model
   218→	if model == "" {
   219→		model = os.Getenv("E2E_GEMINI_MODEL")
   220→		// Default to gemini-2.5-flash if not specified
   221→		if model == "" {
   222→			model = "gemini-2.5-flash"
   223→		}
   224→	}
   225→
   226→	timeout := config.Timeout
   227→	if timeout == 0 {
   228→		if envTimeout := os.Getenv("E2E_TIMEOUT"); envTimeout != "" {
   229→			if parsed, err := time.ParseDuration(envTimeout); err == nil {
   230→				timeout = parsed
   231→			}
   232→		}
   233→		if timeout == 0 {
   234→			timeout = 2 * time.Minute
   235→		}
   236→	}
   237→
   238→	return &GeminiCLIRunner{
   239→		Model:   model,
   240→		Timeout: timeout,
   241→	}
   242→}
   243→
   244→func (r *GeminiCLIRunner) Name() string {
   245→	return AgentNameGemini
   246→}
   247→
   248→// IsAvailable checks if Gemini CLI is installed and authenticated.
   249→func (r *GeminiCLIRunner) IsAvailable() (bool, error) {
   250→	// Check if gemini CLI is in PATH
   251→	if _, err := exec.LookPath("gemini"); err != nil {
   252→		return false, fmt.Errorf("gemini CLI not found in PATH: %w", err)
   253→	}
   254→
   255→	// Check if gemini is working (--version doesn't require auth)
   256→	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 10*time.Second)
   257→	defer cancel()
   258→
   259→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "gemini", "--version")
   260→	if err := cmd.Run(); err != nil {
   261→		return false, fmt.Errorf("gemini CLI not working: %w", err)
   262→	}
   263→
   264→	return true, nil
   265→}
   266→
   267→func (r *GeminiCLIRunner) RunPrompt(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string) (*AgentResult, error) {
   268→	return r.RunPromptWithTools(ctx, workDir, prompt, nil)
   269→}
   270→
   271→func (r *GeminiCLIRunner) RunPromptWithTools(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string, tools []string) (*AgentResult, error) {
   272→	// Build command: gemini -m <model> -p "<prompt>" --approval-mode auto_edit --allowed-tools <tools>
   273→	// --approval-mode auto_edit: auto-approves edit tools (write_file, replace) while prompting for others
   274→	// --allowed-tools: comma-separated list of tools that bypass confirmation (e.g., git commands)
   275→	args := []string{
   276→		"-m", r.Model,
   277→		"-p", prompt,
   278→		"--approval-mode", "auto_edit",
   279→	}
   280→
   281→	// Add default git tools that should be allowed without confirmation
   282→	defaultAllowedTools := []string{
   283→		"ShellTool(git status)",
   284→		"ShellTool(git add)",
   285→		"ShellTool(git commit)",
   286→		"ShellTool(git diff)",
   287→		"ShellTool(git log)",
   288→	}
   289→
   290→	// Merge with any additional tools passed in
   291→	allTools := append(defaultAllowedTools, tools...)
   292→	if len(allTools) > 0 {
   293→		args = append(args, "--allowed-tools", strings.Join(allTools, ","))
   294→	}
   295→
   296→	// Create context with timeout
   297→	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, r.Timeout)
   298→	defer cancel()
   299→
   300→	//nolint:gosec // args are constructed from trusted config, not user input
   301→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "gemini", args...)
   302→	cmd.Dir = workDir
   303→
   304→	var stdout, stderr bytes.Buffer
   305→	cmd.Stdout = &stdout
   306→	cmd.Stderr = &stderr
   307→
   308→	start := time.Now()
   309→	err := cmd.Run()
   310→	duration := time.Since(start)
   311→
   312→	result := &AgentResult{
   313→		Stdout:   stdout.String(),
   314→		Stderr:   stderr.String(),
   315→		Duration: duration,
   316→	}
   317→
   318→	if err != nil {
   319→		exitErr := &exec.ExitError{}
   320→		if errors.As(err, &exitErr) {
   321→			result.ExitCode = exitErr.ExitCode()
   322→		} else {
   323→			result.ExitCode = -1
   324→		}
   325→		//nolint:wrapcheck // error is from exec.Run, caller can check ExitCode in result
   326→		return result, err
   327→	}
   328→
   329→	result.ExitCode = 0
   330→	return result, nil
   331→}

   333→// OpenCodeRunner implements AgentRunner for OpenCode.

   340→// NewOpenCodeRunner creates a new OpenCode runner with the given config.
   341→func NewOpenCodeRunner(config AgentRunnerConfig) *OpenCodeRunner {
   342→	model := config.Model

   345→		// No default model - OpenCode uses whatever is configured in the project
   346→	}
   347→
   348→	timeout := config.Timeout
   349→	if timeout == 0 {

   352→				timeout = parsed
   353→			}
   354→		}
   355→		if timeout == 0 {

   387→func (r *OpenCodeRunner) RunPrompt(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string) (*AgentResult, error) {

   390→
   391→func (r *OpenCodeRunner) RunPromptWithTools(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string, _ []string) (*AgentResult, error) {
   392→	// Build command: opencode run "<prompt>"
   393→	// In non-interactive mode, all permissions are auto-approved.
   394→	args := []string{"run", "--print-logs"}
   395→
   396→	if r.Model != "" {
   397→		args = append(args, "--model", r.Model)
   398→	}
   399→
   400→	args = append(args, prompt)
   401→
   402→	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, r.Timeout)
   403→	defer cancel()
   404→
   405→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "opencode", args...)
   406→	cmd.Dir = workDir
   407→
   408→	var stdout, stderr bytes.Buffer
   409→	cmd.Stdout = &stdout
   410→	cmd.Stderr = &stderr
   411→
   412→	start := time.Now()
   413→	err := cmd.Run()
   414→	duration := time.Since(start)
   415→
   416→	result := &AgentResult{
   417→		Stdout:   stdout.String(),
   418→		Stderr:   stderr.String(),
   419→		Duration: duration,
   420→	}
   421→
   422→	if err != nil {
   423→		exitErr := &exec.ExitError{}
   424→		if errors.As(err, &exitErr) {
   425→			result.ExitCode = exitErr.ExitCode()
   426→		} else {
   427→			result.ExitCode = -1
   428→		}
   429→		//nolint:wrapcheck // error is from exec.Run, caller can check ExitCode in result
   430→		return result, err
   431→	}
   432→
   433→	result.ExitCode = 0
   434→	return result, nil
   435→}

     1→//go:build e2e
     2→
     3→package e2e
     4→
     5→import (
     6→	"context"
     7→	"crypto/sha256"
     8→	"encoding/hex"
     9→	"encoding/json"
    10→	"errors"
    11→	"os"
    12→	"os/exec"
    13→	"path/filepath"
    14→	"strings"
    15→	"testing"
    16→	"time"
    17→
    18→	"github.com/go-git/go-git/v5"
    19→	"github.com/go-git/go-git/v5/plumbing"
    20→	"github.com/go-git/go-git/v5/plumbing/format/config"
    21→	"github.com/go-git/go-git/v5/plumbing/object"
    22→)
    23→
    24→// defaultAgent holds the agent to test with, determined in TestMain.
    25→var defaultAgent string
    26→
    27→// testBinaryPath holds the path to the CLI binary built once in TestMain.
    28→// All tests share this binary to avoid repeated builds.
    29→var testBinaryPath string
    30→
    31→// TestEnv manages an isolated test environment for E2E tests with real agent calls.
    32→type TestEnv struct {
    33→	T       *testing.T
    34→	RepoDir string
    35→	Agent   AgentRunner
    36→}
    37→
    38→// getTestBinary returns the path to the shared test binary.
    39→// It panics if TestMain hasn't run (testBinaryPath is empty).
    40→func getTestBinary() string {
    41→	if testBinaryPath == "" {
    42→		panic("testBinaryPath not set - TestMain must run before tests")
    43→	}
    44→	return testBinaryPath
    45→}
    46→
    47→// NewTestEnv creates a new isolated E2E test environment.
    48→func NewTestEnv(t *testing.T) *TestEnv {
    49→	t.Helper()
    50→
    51→	// Resolve symlinks on macOS where /var -> /private/var
    52→	repoDir := t.TempDir()
    53→	if resolved, err := filepath.EvalSymlinks(repoDir); err == nil {
    54→		repoDir = resolved
    55→	}
    56→
    57→	// Create agent runner
    58→	agent := NewAgentRunner(defaultAgent, AgentRunnerConfig{})
    59→
    60→	return &TestEnv{
    61→		T:       t,
    62→		RepoDir: repoDir,
    63→		Agent:   agent,
    64→	}
    65→}
    66→
    67→// NewFeatureBranchEnv creates an E2E test environment ready for testing.
    68→// It initializes the repo, creates an initial commit on main,
    69→// checks out a feature branch, and sets up agent hooks.
    70→func NewFeatureBranchEnv(t *testing.T, strategyName string) *TestEnv {
    71→	t.Helper()
    72→
    73→	env := NewTestEnv(t)
    74→	env.InitRepo()
    75→	env.WriteFile("README.md", "# Test Repository\n\nThis is a test repository for E2E testing.\n")
    76→	env.GitAdd("README.md")
    77→	env.GitCommit("Initial commit")
    78→	env.GitCheckoutNewBranch("feature/e2e-test")
    79→
    80→	// OpenCode's `run` (non-interactive) mode defaults external_directory permission
    81→	// to "ask", which auto-rejects since there's no user to prompt — even when the
    82→	// path is the project directory itself. Override to "allow" for the test repo.
    83→	// Note: specific path patterns don't work here because OpenCode evaluates the
    84→	// catch-all "ask" rule before specific "allow" rules (known issue).
    85→	if defaultAgent == AgentNameOpenCode {
    86→		env.WriteFile("opencode.json", `{
    87→  "permission": {
    88→    "external_directory": "allow"
    89→  }
    90→}
    91→`)
    92→	}
    93→
    94→	// Use `entire enable` to set up everything (hooks, settings, etc.)
    95→	// This sets up .entire/settings.json and .claude/settings.json with hooks
    96→	env.RunEntireEnable(strategyName)
    97→
    98→	// Commit all files created by `entire enable` so they survive git stash -u operations.
    99→	// Without this, stash operations would stash away the hooks config and entire settings,
   100→	// causing hooks to not fire for subsequent prompts and stash pop conflicts.
   101→	// Using "git add ." is safe in test repos since we control all content.
   102→	env.GitAddAll()
   103→	env.GitCommit("Add entire and agent config")
   104→
   105→	return env
   106→}
   107→
   108→// RunEntireEnable runs `entire enable` to set up the project with hooks.
   109→// Uses the configured defaultAgent (from E2E_AGENT env var or "claude-code").
   110→func (env *TestEnv) RunEntireEnable(strategyName string) {
   111→	env.T.Helper()
   112→
   113→	args := []string{
   114→		"enable",
   115→		"--agent", defaultAgent,
   116→		"--strategy", strategyName,
   117→		"--telemetry=false",
   118→		"--force", // Force reinstall hooks in case they exist
   119→	}
   120→
   121→	//nolint:gosec,noctx // test code, args are static
   122→	cmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), args...)
   123→	cmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   124→
   125→	output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
   126→	if err != nil {
   127→		env.T.Fatalf("entire enable failed: %v\nOutput: %s", err, output)
   128→	}
   129→	env.T.Logf("entire enable output: %s", output)
   130→}
   131→
   132→// InitRepo initializes a git repository in the test environment.
   133→func (env *TestEnv) InitRepo() {
   134→	env.T.Helper()
   135→
   136→	repo, err := git.PlainInit(env.RepoDir, false)
   137→	if err != nil {
   138→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to init git repo: %v", err)
   139→	}
   140→
   141→	// Configure git user for commits
   142→	cfg, err := repo.Config()
   143→	if err != nil {
   144→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get repo config: %v", err)
   145→	}
   146→	cfg.User.Name = "E2E Test User"
   147→	cfg.User.Email = "e2e-test@example.com"
   148→
   149→	// Disable GPG signing for test commits
   150→	if cfg.Raw == nil {
   151→		cfg.Raw = config.New()
   152→	}
   153→	cfg.Raw.Section("commit").SetOption("gpgsign", "false")
   154→
   155→	if err := repo.SetConfig(cfg); err != nil {
   156→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to set repo config: %v", err)
   157→	}
   158→}
   159→
   160→// WriteFile creates a file with the given content in the test repo.

   171→	}
   172→
   173→	//nolint:gosec // test code, permissions are intentionally standard
   174→	if err := os.WriteFile(fullPath, []byte(content), 0o644); err != nil {

   176→	}
   177→}

   272→// GitCommitWithShadowHooks stages and commits files, running the prepare-commit-msg
   273→// and post-commit hooks like a real workflow.
   274→func (env *TestEnv) GitCommitWithShadowHooks(message string, files ...string) {
   275→	env.T.Helper()
   276→
   277→	// Stage files using go-git
   278→	for _, file := range files {
   279→		env.GitAdd(file)
   280→	}
   281→
   282→	// Create a temp file for the commit message
   283→	msgFile := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".git", "COMMIT_EDITMSG")
   284→	//nolint:gosec // test code, permissions are intentionally standard
   285→	if err := os.WriteFile(msgFile, []byte(message), 0o644); err != nil {
   286→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to write commit message file: %v", err)
   287→	}
   288→
   289→	// Run prepare-commit-msg hook
   290→	//nolint:gosec,noctx // test code, args are from trusted test setup, no context needed
   291→	prepCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "prepare-commit-msg", msgFile, "message")
   292→	prepCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   293→	prepCmd.Env = append(os.Environ(),
   294→		"ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1",
   295→		"ENTIRE_TEST_CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR="+filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".claude"),
   296→		"ENTIRE_TEST_GEMINI_PROJECT_DIR="+filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".gemini"),
   297→	)
   298→	if output, err := prepCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
   299→		env.T.Logf("prepare-commit-msg output: %s", output)
   300→	}
   301→
   302→	// Read the modified message
   303→	//nolint:gosec // test code, path is from test setup
   304→	modifiedMsg, err := os.ReadFile(msgFile)
   305→	if err != nil {
   306→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to read modified commit message: %v", err)
   307→	}
   308→
   309→	// Create the commit using go-git with the modified message
   310→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   311→	if err != nil {
   312→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   313→	}
   314→
   315→	worktree, err := repo.Worktree()
   316→	if err != nil {
   317→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get worktree: %v", err)
   318→	}
   319→
   320→	_, err = worktree.Commit(string(modifiedMsg), &git.CommitOptions{
   321→		Author: &object.Signature{
   322→			Name:  "E2E Test User",
   323→			Email: "e2e-test@example.com",
   324→			When:  time.Now(),
   325→		},
   326→	})
   327→	if err != nil {
   328→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to commit: %v", err)
   329→	}
   330→
   331→	// Run post-commit hook
   332→	//nolint:gosec,noctx // test code, args are from trusted test setup, no context needed
   333→	postCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "post-commit")
   334→	postCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   335→	postCmd.Env = append(os.Environ(),
   336→		"ENTIRE_TEST_CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR="+filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".claude"),
   337→		"ENTIRE_TEST_GEMINI_PROJECT_DIR="+filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".gemini"),
   338→	)
   339→	if output, err := postCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
   340→		env.T.Logf("post-commit output: %s", output)
   341→	}
   342→}

   425→// GitCommitWithTrailerRemoved stages and commits files, simulating what happens when
   426→// a user removes the Entire-Checkpoint trailer during commit message editing.
   427→// This tests the opt-out behavior where removing the trailer skips condensation.
   428→func (env *TestEnv) GitCommitWithTrailerRemoved(message string, files ...string) {
   429→	env.T.Helper()
   430→
   431→	// Stage files using go-git
   432→	for _, file := range files {
   433→		env.GitAdd(file)
   434→	}
   435→
   436→	// Create a temp file for the commit message
   437→	msgFile := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".git", "COMMIT_EDITMSG")
   438→	//nolint:gosec // test code, permissions are intentionally standard
   439→	if err := os.WriteFile(msgFile, []byte(message), 0o644); err != nil {
   440→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to write commit message file: %v", err)
   441→	}
   442→
   443→	// Run prepare-commit-msg hook
   444→	//nolint:gosec,noctx // test code, args are from trusted test setup, no context needed
   445→	prepCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "prepare-commit-msg", msgFile)
   446→	prepCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   447→	prepCmd.Env = append(os.Environ(),
   448→		"ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1",
   449→		"ENTIRE_TEST_CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR="+filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".claude"),
   450→		"ENTIRE_TEST_GEMINI_PROJECT_DIR="+filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".gemini"),
   451→	)
   452→	if output, err := prepCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
   453→		env.T.Logf("prepare-commit-msg output: %s", output)
   454→	}
   455→
   456→	// Read the modified message (with trailer added by hook)
   457→	//nolint:gosec // test code, path is from test setup
   458→	modifiedMsg, err := os.ReadFile(msgFile)
   459→	if err != nil {
   460→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to read modified commit message: %v", err)
   461→	}
   462→
   463→	// REMOVE the Entire-Checkpoint trailer (simulating user editing the message)
   464→	lines := strings.Split(string(modifiedMsg), "\n")
   465→	var cleanedLines []string
   466→	for _, line := range lines {
   467→		if strings.HasPrefix(line, "Entire-Checkpoint:") {
   468→			continue
   469→		}
   470→		if strings.Contains(line, "Remove the Entire-Checkpoint trailer") {
   471→			continue
   472→		}
   473→		if strings.Contains(line, "trailer will be added to your next commit") {
   474→			continue
   475→		}
   476→		cleanedLines = append(cleanedLines, line)
   477→	}
   478→	cleanedMsg := strings.TrimRight(strings.Join(cleanedLines, "\n"), "\n") + "\n"
   479→
   480→	// Create the commit using go-git with the cleaned message (no trailer)
   481→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   482→	if err != nil {
   483→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   484→	}
   485→
   486→	worktree, err := repo.Worktree()
   487→	if err != nil {
   488→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get worktree: %v", err)
   489→	}
   490→
   491→	_, err = worktree.Commit(cleanedMsg, &git.CommitOptions{
   492→		Author: &object.Signature{
   493→			Name:  "E2E Test User",
   494→			Email: "e2e-test@example.com",
   495→			When:  time.Now(),
   496→		},
   497→	})
   498→	if err != nil {
   499→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to commit: %v", err)
   500→	}
   501→
   502→	// Run post-commit hook - since trailer was removed, no condensation should happen
   503→	//nolint:gosec,noctx // test code, args are from trusted test setup, no context needed
   504→	postCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "post-commit")
   505→	postCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   506→	postCmd.Env = append(os.Environ(),
   507→		"ENTIRE_TEST_CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR="+filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".claude"),
   508→		"ENTIRE_TEST_GEMINI_PROJECT_DIR="+filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".gemini"),
   509→	)
   510→	if output, err := postCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
   511→		env.T.Logf("post-commit output: %s", output)
   512→	}
   513→}

   543→
   544→// RewindPoint mirrors the rewind --list JSON output.
   545→type RewindPoint struct {
   546→	ID               string    `json:"id"`
   547→	Message          string    `json:"message"`
   548→	MetadataDir      string    `json:"metadata_dir"`
   549→	Date             time.Time `json:"date"`
   550→	IsTaskCheckpoint bool      `json:"is_task_checkpoint"`
   551→	ToolUseID        string    `json:"tool_use_id"`
   552→	IsLogsOnly       bool      `json:"is_logs_only"`
   553→	CondensationID   string    `json:"condensation_id"`
   554→}
   555→
   556→// GetRewindPoints returns available rewind points using the CLI.
   557→func (env *TestEnv) GetRewindPoints() []RewindPoint {
   558→	env.T.Helper()
   559→
   560→	//nolint:gosec,noctx // test code, args are static, no context needed
   561→	cmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "rewind", "--list")
   562→	cmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   563→
   564→	output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
   565→	if err != nil {
   566→		env.T.Fatalf("rewind --list failed: %v\nOutput: %s", err, output)
   567→	}
   568→
   569→	// Parse JSON output
   570→	var jsonPoints []struct {
   571→		ID               string `json:"id"`
   572→		Message          string `json:"message"`
   573→		MetadataDir      string `json:"metadata_dir"`
   574→		Date             string `json:"date"`
   575→		IsTaskCheckpoint bool   `json:"is_task_checkpoint"`
   576→		ToolUseID        string `json:"tool_use_id"`
   577→		IsLogsOnly       bool   `json:"is_logs_only"`
   578→		CondensationID   string `json:"condensation_id"`
   579→	}
   580→
   581→	if err := json.Unmarshal(output, &jsonPoints); err != nil {
   582→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to parse rewind points: %v\nOutput: %s", err, output)
   583→	}
   584→
   585→	points := make([]RewindPoint, len(jsonPoints))
   586→	for i, jp := range jsonPoints {
   587→		//nolint:errcheck // date parsing failure is acceptable, defaults to zero time
   588→		date, _ := time.Parse(time.RFC3339, jp.Date)
   589→		points[i] = RewindPoint{
   590→			ID:               jp.ID,
   591→			Message:          jp.Message,
   592→			MetadataDir:      jp.MetadataDir,
   593→			Date:             date,
   594→			IsTaskCheckpoint: jp.IsTaskCheckpoint,
   595→			ToolUseID:        jp.ToolUseID,
   596→			IsLogsOnly:       jp.IsLogsOnly,
   597→			CondensationID:   jp.CondensationID,
   598→		}
   599→	}
   600→
   601→	return points
   602→}
